U
I want to develop an assistive wearable project for visually impaired people using **only two hardware components: an ESP32 and an MPU6050**.

### Project Goal

Build a wearable system that uses the MPU6050's **3-axis accelerometer and 3-axis gyroscope** to recognize intentional hand/body gestures and detect falls. The ESP32 should process the MPU6050 data and communicate with a **Blynk IoT mobile application** over Wi-Fi.

**Do not add any other physical sensors.** The MPU6050 must be the only sensor used.

### Main Functions

#### 1. Gesture Recognition

Design a simple set of intuitive gestures that a visually impaired person can easily perform.

For example:

* Tilt/Move Right → Next
* Tilt/Move Left → Previous
* Tilt Forward → Select/Confirm
* Tilt Backward → Cancel/Back
* Single Shake → Repeat/Activate
* Double Shake → Emergency/important action

The gestures should be:

* Easy to perform
* Distinct from normal walking movements
* Resistant to accidental triggering
* Customizable if possible

Explain how the accelerometer and gyroscope data can be used together to distinguish gestures.

#### 2. Fall Detection

Use only MPU6050 data to detect a possible fall.

The algorithm should not simply trigger when acceleration becomes high. It should consider multiple conditions such as:

* Sudden acceleration/impact
* Sudden orientation change
* Gyroscope activity
* Post-impact inactivity
* Duration of the event

Design a reliable fall-detection algorithm that minimizes false positives during normal walking, sitting, running, or intentional gestures.

Include a short confirmation period after a suspected fall before generating an alert.

#### 3. Activity Recognition

If possible, use the same MPU6050 data to distinguish basic activities such as:

* Standing
* Walking
* Sitting
* Running
* Falling
* Idle/inactive

Explain whether a rule-based algorithm or TinyML model would be more appropriate.

### Blynk IoT Integration

Use **Blynk IoT** as the phone interface.

ESP32 should connect to Blynk through Wi-Fi.

Architecture:

MPU6050 → ESP32 → Wi-Fi → Blynk Cloud → Blynk Mobile App

The Blynk application should provide:

* Current activity
* Detected gesture
* Fall status
* Accelerometer X/Y/Z
* Gyroscope X/Y/Z
* Device connection status
* Event history
* Fall alerts/notifications

For example:

**Normal**

> Activity: Walking
> Fall Status: Normal
> Last Gesture: Right

**Gesture detected**

> Gesture: Forward
> Action: Confirm

**Fall detected**

> ⚠️ Possible Fall Detected
> Please check the user.

### Important Requirement for Visually Impaired Users

The system should not depend on visually reading the Blynk dashboard.

Design the Blynk phone-side feedback so important events can be communicated through **phone notifications and/or text-to-speech/accessibility features**.

For example:

ESP32 detects:
`GESTURE_NEXT`

Blynk/phone feedback:

> "Next."

ESP32 detects:
`FALL_DETECTED`

Phone feedback:

> "Possible fall detected. Please check the device."

Keep the feedback simple and understandable.

### Technical Requirements

Please provide:

1. Complete project concept
2. Real-world problem statement
3. Proposed solution
4. System architecture/block diagram
5. ESP32 + MPU6050 wiring
6. MPU6050 calibration procedure
7. Accelerometer and gyroscope data processing
8. Gesture-recognition algorithm
9. Fall-detection algorithm
10. Activity-recognition algorithm
11. Methods to reduce false gesture/fall detection
12. Blynk IoT architecture
13. Blynk Template/Data Streams/Virtual Pin design
14. Blynk dashboard layout
15. Blynk event and notification configuration
16. ESP32 Arduino code
17. Blynk integration code
18. Recommended sampling rate
19. Filtering method
20. Threshold-selection/calibration procedure
21. Testing procedure
22. Test cases for gestures, normal activities, and falls
23. Possible future improvements

### Hardware Restriction

The final prototype must contain only:

* ESP32
* MPU6050

No ultrasonic sensor, ToF sensor, GPS, GSM module, additional IMU, camera, or other physical sensor should be added.

The smartphone and Blynk IoT are allowed only as the **communication, user-interface, notification, and feedback layer**.

Make the project technically realistic and suitable for an **embedded systems engineering project**, not just a basic MPU6050 demonstration.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Create a new Arduino-framework project for the ESP32-C3-DevKitM-1 using only the ESP32 and MPU6050. Use GPIO4 for I2C SDA and GPIO5 for I2C SCL, 3.3 V power, and GND; do not use restricted GPIO11–17.
2. Implement a nonblocking MPU6050 driver with address detection at 0x68 and 0x69, WHO_AM_I validation, 100 Hz sampling, ±8 g accelerometer range, ±1000 dps gyroscope range, I2C timeout handling, and burst reads.
3. Add startup and user-requested calibration. The default calibration holds the device still for 5 seconds to estimate gyro bias and accelerometer 1 g normalization, stores valid calibration data in ESP32 NVS, and documents optional six-position accelerometer calibration for improved accuracy.
4. Process samples with timestamped records, low-pass filtered gravity, high-pass dynamic acceleration, gyro bias correction, acceleration magnitude, jerk, angular velocity, and a complementary roll/pitch estimate. Use a fixed-rate loop based on micros() and avoid blocking delays.
5. Implement a gesture state machine for relative right, left, forward, and backward tilts, plus single- and double-shake recognition. Require direction, angular velocity, duration, return-to-neutral behavior, confidence, cooldown, and walking-motion rejection before publishing an action.
6. Implement rule-based activity recognition over approximately 2-second windows. Classify idle, standing, walking, running, and sitting using acceleration variance, gyro variance, dynamic acceleration, periodicity, cadence, and calibrated posture. Report confidence and document that sitting versus standing is heuristic and depends strongly on consistent wrist, upper-arm, or torso mounting.
7. Implement a fall state machine with NORMAL, CANDIDATE, CONFIRMING, and CONFIRMED states. Combine optional free-fall, impact, orientation change, gyro activity, post-impact inactivity, event duration, and activity context; never trigger a fall from acceleration magnitude alone. Use an 8-second confirmation period, cancel false candidates when normal activity resumes, and permit acknowledgement through a deliberate cancel gesture or Blynk control.
8. Add Blynk IoT station-mode integration with credentials stored outside source control. Define datastreams for activity, confidence, gesture, action, fall status, fall score, accelerometer XYZ, gyroscope XYZ, Wi-Fi RSSI, connection state, event history, fall acknowledgement, and calibration control. Publish raw telemetry at approximately 1 Hz and publish status changes or recognized events immediately while respecting Blynk rate limits.
9. Integrate sensor processing, recognizers, Blynk callbacks, reconnection handling, and event queuing in the main Arduino sketch. Keep local detection operational while Wi-Fi is unavailable, use bounded reconnect attempts with backoff, and queue only recent critical events for delivery after reconnection.
10. Configure Blynk events for confirmed falls, emergency double shakes, and gesture announcements. Use concise notification text such as Next., Confirm., Back., Possible fall detected. Please check the device., and Emergency gesture detected. Document Android TalkBack, iOS VoiceOver, and notification read-aloud configuration because Blynk notifications alone do not guarantee text-to-speech.
11. Document the problem statement, solution, architecture diagram, wiring, calibration, filtering, algorithms, thresholds, Blynk template setup, dashboard layout, accessibility configuration, limitations, test procedure, test cases, and future TinyML improvements in README.md. State clearly that the prototype is not a certified medical or emergency-response device.
12. Build and test the firmware with safe, controlled motion trials, record labeled feature data, measure gesture false positives, fall detection latency, activity accuracy, Wi-Fi recovery, sensor disconnect behavior, and Blynk notification delivery. Tune thresholds only after evidence from the target mounting position and users.
13. Keep rule-based recognition as the initial implementation because it is explainable, memory-efficient, and does not require a training dataset. Consider a later TinyML classifier only if labeled MPU6050 data demonstrates that the rule-based approach cannot meet the required accuracy.

**Alternatives:** If the MPU6050 is not detected, inspect 3.3 V and GND, run the 0x68/0x69 scanner, and check the module AD0 setting; a compatible Arduino MPU6050 library can replace the direct register driver without changing GPIO4/GPIO5. If Blynk station-mode connectivity or TLS fails, retain local recognition and use a supported Blynk ESP32 library version or a Wi-Fi client wrapper while preserving the same virtual-pin and event interface. If rule-based activity or fall detection remains inaccurate after labeled tuning, collect more MPU6050-only data and add a small feature-based TinyML classifier; do not add physical sensors. If Wi-Fi is unavailable, show the local event state over serial, retain recent critical events with expiration, and deliver them only after reconnection rather than blocking sensor processing.

// ===== main/i2c_basic_example_main.c =====
/*
 * SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2024 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD
 *
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Unlicense OR CC0-1.0
 */
/* i2c - Simple Example

   Simple I2C example that shows how to initialize I2C
   as well as reading and writing from and to registers for a sensor connected over I2C.

   The sensor used in this example is a MPU9250 inertial measurement unit.
*/
#include <stdio.h>
#include "sdkconfig.h"
#include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
#include "freertos/task.h"
#include "esp_log.h"
#include "driver/i2c_master.h"

static const char *TAG = "example";

#define I2C_MASTER_SCL_IO           CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SCL       /*!< GPIO number used for I2C master clock */
#define I2C_MASTER_SDA_IO           CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SDA       /*!< GPIO number used for I2C master data  */
#define I2C_MASTER_NUM              I2C_NUM_0                   /*!< I2C port number for master dev */
#define I2C_MASTER_FREQ_HZ          CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_FREQUENCY /*!< I2C master clock frequency */
#define I2C_MASTER_TX_BUF_DISABLE   0                           /*!< I2C master doesn't need buffer */
#define I2C_MASTER_RX_BUF_DISABLE   0                           /*!< I2C master doesn't need buffer */
#define I2C_MASTER_TIMEOUT_MS       1000

#define MPU9250_SENSOR_ADDR         0x68        /*!< Address of the MPU9250 sensor */
#define MPU9250_WHO_AM_I_REG_ADDR   0x75        /*!< Register addresses of the "who am I" register */
#define MPU9250_PWR_MGMT_1_REG_ADDR 0x6B        /*!< Register addresses of the power management register */
#define MPU9250_RESET_BIT           7

/**
 * @brief Read a sequence of bytes from a MPU9250 sensor registers
 */
static esp_err_t mpu9250_register_read(i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev_handle, uint8_t reg_addr, uint8_t *data, size_t len)
{
    return i2c_master_transmit_receive(dev_handle, &reg_addr, 1, data, len, I2C_MASTER_TIMEOUT_MS / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
}

/**
 * @brief Write a byte to a MPU9250 sensor register
 */
static esp_err_t mpu9250_register_write_byte(i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev_handle, uint8_t reg_addr, uint8_t data)
{
    uint8_t write_buf[2] = {reg_addr, data};
    return i2c_master_transmit(dev_handle, write_buf, sizeof(write_buf), I2C_MASTER_TIMEOUT_MS / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
}

/**
 * @brief i2c master initialization
 */
static void i2c_master_init(i2c_master_bus_handle_t *bus_handle, i2c_master_dev_handle_t *dev_handle)
{
    i2c_master_bus_config_t bus_config = {
        .i2c_port = I2C_MASTER_NUM,
        .sda_io_num = I2C_MASTER_SDA_IO,
        .scl_io_num = I2C_MASTER_SCL_IO,
        .clk_source = I2C_CLK_SRC_DEFAULT,
        .glitch_ignore_cnt = 7,
        .flags.enable_internal_pullup = true,
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_new_master_bus(&bus_config, bus_handle));

    i2c_device_config_t dev_config = {
        .dev_addr_length = I2C_ADDR_BIT_LEN_7,
        .device_address = MPU9250_SENSOR_ADDR,
        .scl_speed_hz = I2C_MASTER_FREQ_HZ,
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_master_bus_add_device(*bus_handle, &dev_config, dev_handle));
}

void app_main(void)
{
    uint8_t data[2];
    i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus_handle;
    i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev_handle;
    i2c_master_init(&bus_handle, &dev_handle);
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "I2C initialized successfully");

    /* Read the MPU9250 WHO_AM_I register, on power up the register should have the value 0x71 */
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(mpu9250_register_read(dev_handle, MPU9250_WHO_AM_I_REG_ADDR, data, 1));
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "WHO_AM_I = %X", data[0]);

    /* Demonstrate writing by resetting the MPU9250 */
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(mpu9250_register_write_byte(dev_handle, MPU9250_PWR_MGMT_1_REG_ADDR, 1 << MPU9250_RESET_BIT));

    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_master_bus_rm_device(dev_handle));
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(i2c_del_master_bus(bus_handle));
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "I2C de-initialized successfully");
}

// ===== main/station_example_main.c =====
/* WiFi station Example

   This example code is in the Public Domain (or CC0 licensed, at your option.)

   Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, this
   software is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS, WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR
   CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
*/
#include <string.h>
#include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
#include "freertos/task.h"
#include "freertos/event_groups.h"
#include "esp_system.h"
#include "esp_wifi.h"
#include "esp_event.h"
#include "esp_log.h"
#include "nvs_flash.h"

#include "lwip/err.h"
#include "lwip/sys.h"

/* The examples use WiFi configuration that you can set via project configuration menu

   If you'd rather not, just change the below entries to strings with
   the config you want - ie #define EXAMPLE_WIFI_SSID "mywifissid"
*/
#define EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_SSID      CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_SSID
#define EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_PASS      CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_PASSWORD
#define EXAMPLE_ESP_MAXIMUM_RETRY  CONFIG_ESP_MAXIMUM_RETRY

#if CONFIG_ESP_STATION_EXAMPLE_WPA3_SAE_PWE_HUNT_AND_PECK
#define ESP_WIFI_SAE_MODE WPA3_SAE_PWE_HUNT_AND_PECK
#define EXAMPLE_H2E_IDENTIFIER ""
#elif CONFIG_ESP_STATION_EXAMPLE_WPA3_SAE_PWE_HASH_TO_ELEMENT
#define ESP_WIFI_SAE_MODE WPA3_SAE_PWE_HASH_TO_ELEMENT
#define EXAMPLE_H2E_IDENTIFIER CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_PW_ID
#elif CONFIG_ESP_STATION_EXAMPLE_WPA3_SAE_PWE_BOTH
#define ESP_WIFI_SAE_MODE WPA3_SAE_PWE_BOTH
#define EXAMPLE_H2E_IDENTIFIER CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_PW_ID
#endif
#if CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_OPEN
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_OPEN
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WEP
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WEP
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WPA_PSK
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WPA_PSK
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WPA2_PSK
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WPA2_PSK
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WPA_WPA2_PSK
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WPA_WPA2_PSK
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WPA3_PSK
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WPA3_PSK
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WPA2_WPA3_PSK
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WPA2_WPA3_PSK
#elif CONFIG_ESP_WIFI_AUTH_WAPI_PSK
#define ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD WIFI_AUTH_WAPI_PSK
#endif

/* FreeRTOS event group to signal when we are connected*/
static EventGroupHandle_t s_wifi_event_group;

/* The event group allows multiple bits for each event, but we only care about two events:
 * - we are connected to the AP with an IP
 * - we failed to connect after the maximum amount of retries */
#define WIFI_CONNECTED_BIT BIT0
#define WIFI_FAIL_BIT      BIT1

static const char *TAG = "wifi station";

static int s_retry_num = 0;


static void event_handler(void* arg, esp_event_base_t event_base,
                                int32_t event_id, void* event_data)
{
    if (event_base == WIFI_EVENT && event_id == WIFI_EVENT_STA_START) {
        esp_wifi_connect();
    } else if (event_base == WIFI_EVENT && event_id == WIFI_EVENT_STA_DISCONNECTED) {
        if (s_retry_num < EXAMPLE_ESP_MAXIMUM_RETRY) {
            esp_wifi_connect();
            s_retry_num++;
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "retry to connect to the AP");
        } else {
            xEventGroupSetBits(s_wifi_event_group, WIFI_FAIL_BIT);
        }
        ESP_LOGI(TAG,"connect to the AP fail");
    } else if (event_base == IP_EVENT && event_id == IP_EVENT_STA_GOT_IP) {
        ip_event_got_ip_t* event = (ip_event_got_ip_t*) event_data;
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "got ip:" IPSTR, IP2STR(&event->ip_info.ip));
        s_retry_num = 0;
        xEventGroupSetBits(s_wifi_event_group, WIFI_CONNECTED_BIT);
    }
}

void wifi_init_sta(void)
{
    s_wifi_event_group = xEventGroupCreate();

    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_netif_init());

    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_event_loop_create_default());
    esp_netif_create_default_wifi_sta();

    wifi_init_config_t cfg = WIFI_INIT_CONFIG_DEFAULT();
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_init(&cfg));

    esp_event_handler_instance_t instance_any_id;
    esp_event_handler_instance_t instance_got_ip;
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_event_handler_instance_register(WIFI_EVENT,
                                                        ESP_EVENT_ANY_ID,
                                                        &event_handler,
                                                        NULL,
                                                        &instance_any_id));
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_event_handler_instance_register(IP_EVENT,
                                                        IP_EVENT_STA_GOT_IP,
                                                        &event_handler,
                                                        NULL,
                                                        &instance_got_ip));

    wifi_config_t wifi_config = {
        .sta = {
            .ssid = EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_SSID,
            .password = EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_PASS,
            /* Authmode threshold resets to WPA2 as default if password matches WPA2 standards (password len => 8).
             * If you want to connect the device to deprecated WEP/WPA networks, Please set the threshold value
             * to WIFI_AUTH_WEP/WIFI_AUTH_WPA_PSK and set the password with length and format matching to
             * WIFI_AUTH_WEP/WIFI_AUTH_WPA_PSK standards.
             */
            .threshold.authmode = ESP_WIFI_SCAN_AUTH_MODE_THRESHOLD,
            .sae_pwe_h2e = ESP_WIFI_SAE_MODE,
            .sae_h2e_identifier = EXAMPLE_H2E_IDENTIFIER,
        },
    };
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_set_mode(WIFI_MODE_STA) );
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_set_config(WIFI_IF_STA, &wifi_config) );
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_start() );

    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "wifi_init_sta finished.");

    /* Waiting until either the connection is established (WIFI_CONNECTED_BIT) or connection failed for the maximum
     * number of re-tries (WIFI_FAIL_BIT). The bits are set by event_handler() (see above) */
    EventBits_t bits = xEventGroupWaitBits(s_wifi_event_group,
            WIFI_CONNECTED_BIT | WIFI_FAIL_BIT,
            pdFALSE,
            pdFALSE,
            portMAX_DELAY);

    /* xEventGroupWaitBits() returns the bits before the call returned, hence we can test which event actually
     * happened. */
    if (bits & WIFI_CONNECTED_BIT) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "connected to ap SSID:%s password:%s",
                 EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_SSID, EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_PASS);
    } else if (bits & WIFI_FAIL_BIT) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Failed to connect to SSID:%s, password:%s",
                 EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_SSID, EXAMPLE_ESP_WIFI_PASS);
    } else {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "UNEXPECTED EVENT");
    }
}

void app_main(void)
{
    //Initialize NVS
    esp_err_t ret = nvs_flash_init();
    if (ret == ESP_ERR_NVS_NO_FREE_PAGES || ret == ESP_ERR_NVS_NEW_VERSION_FOUND) {
      ESP_ERROR_CHECK(nvs_flash_erase());
      ret = nvs_flash_init();
    }
    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(ret);

    if (CONFIG_LOG_MAXIMUM_LEVEL > CONFIG_LOG_DEFAULT_LEVEL) {
        /* If you only want to open more logs in the wifi module, you need to make the max level greater than the default level,
         * and call esp_log_level_set() before esp_wifi_init() to improve the log level of the wifi module. */
        esp_log_level_set("wifi", CONFIG_LOG_MAXIMUM_LEVEL);
    }

    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "ESP_WIFI_MODE_STA");
    wifi_init_sta();
}

// ===== main/https_request_example_main.c =====
/*
 * HTTPS GET Example using plain Mbed TLS sockets
 *
 * Contacts the howsmyssl.com API via TLS v1.2 and reads a JSON
 * response.
 *
 * Adapted from the ssl_client1 example in Mbed TLS.
 *
 * SPDX-FileCopyrightText: The Mbed TLS Contributors
 *
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
 *
 * SPDX-FileContributor: 2015-2025 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD
 */

#include <string.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <inttypes.h>
#include <time.h>
#include <sys/time.h>
#include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
#include "freertos/task.h"
#include "freertos/event_groups.h"
#include "esp_wifi.h"
#include "esp_event.h"
#include "esp_log.h"
#include "esp_system.h"
#include "esp_timer.h"
#include "nvs_flash.h"
#include "nvs.h"
#include "protocol_examples_common.h"
#include "esp_sntp.h"
#include "esp_netif.h"

#include "lwip/err.h"
#include "lwip/sockets.h"
#include "lwip/sys.h"
#include "lwip/netdb.h"
#include "lwip/dns.h"

#include "esp_tls.h"
#include "sdkconfig.h"
#if CONFIG_MBEDTLS_CERTIFICATE_BUNDLE && CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS
#include "esp_crt_bundle.h"
#endif
#include "time_sync.h"

/* Constants that aren't configurable in menuconfig */
#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_SSL_PROTO_TLS1_3_CLIENT
#define WEB_SERVER "tls13.browserleaks.com"
#define WEB_PORT "443"
#define WEB_URL "https://tls13.browserleaks.com/tls"
#else
#define WEB_SERVER "howsmyssl.com"
#define WEB_PORT "443"
#define WEB_URL "https://www.howsmyssl.com/a/check"
#endif

#define SERVER_URL_MAX_SZ 256

static const char *TAG = "example";

/* Timer interval once every day (24 Hours) */
#define TIME_PERIOD (86400000000ULL)

static const char HOWSMYSSL_REQUEST[] = "GET " WEB_URL " HTTP/1.1\r\n"
                             "Host: "WEB_SERVER"\r\n"
                             "User-Agent: esp-idf/1.0 esp32\r\n"
                             "\r\n";

#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_CLIENT_SESSION_TICKETS
static const char LOCAL_SRV_REQUEST[] = "GET " CONFIG_EXAMPLE_LOCAL_SERVER_URL " HTTP/1.1\r\n"
                             "Host: "WEB_SERVER"\r\n"
                             "User-Agent: esp-idf/1.0 esp32\r\n"
                             "\r\n";
#endif

/* Root cert for howsmyssl.com, taken from server_root_cert.pem

   The PEM file was extracted from the output of this command:
   openssl s_client -showcerts -connect www.howsmyssl.com:443 </dev/null

   The CA root cert is the last cert given in the chain of certs.

   To embed it in the app binary, the PEM file is named
   in the component.mk COMPONENT_EMBED_TXTFILES variable.
*/
extern const uint8_t server_root_cert_pem_start[] asm("_binary_server_root_cert_pem_start");
extern const uint8_t server_root_cert_pem_end[]   asm("_binary_server_root_cert_pem_end");

extern const uint8_t local_server_cert_pem_start[] asm("_binary_local_server_cert_pem_start");
extern const uint8_t local_server_cert_pem_end[]   asm("_binary_local_server_cert_pem_end");
#if CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS
#if defined(CONFIG_EXAMPLE_SSL_PROTO_TLS1_3_CLIENT)
static const int server_supported_ciphersuites[] = {MBEDTLS_TLS1_3_AES_256_GCM_SHA384, MBEDTLS_TLS1_3_AES_128_CCM_SHA256, 0};
static const int server_unsupported_ciphersuites[] = {MBEDTLS_TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_ARIA_128_CBC_SHA256, 0};
#else
static const int server_supported_ciphersuites[] = {MBEDTLS_TLS_RSA_WITH_AES_256_GCM_SHA384, MBEDTLS_TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_AES_128_CBC_SHA256, 0};
static const int server_unsupported_ciphersuites[] = {MBEDTLS_TLS_ECDHE_RSA_WITH_ARIA_128_CBC_SHA256, 0};
#endif // CONFIG_EXAMPLE_SSL_PROTO_TLS1_3_CLIENT
#endif // CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS

#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_CLIENT_SESSION_TICKETS
static esp_tls_client_session_t *tls_client_session = NULL;
static bool save_client_session = false;
#endif

static void https_get_request(esp_tls_cfg_t cfg, const char *WEB_SERVER_URL, const char *REQUEST)
{
    char buf[512];
    int ret, len;

    esp_tls_t *tls = esp_tls_init();
    if (!tls) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Failed to allocate esp_tls handle!");
        goto exit;
    }

    if (esp_tls_conn_http_new_sync(WEB_SERVER_URL, &cfg, tls) == 1) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Connection established...");
    } else {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Connection failed...");
        int esp_tls_code = 0, esp_tls_flags = 0;
        esp_tls_error_handle_t tls_e = NULL;
        esp_tls_get_error_handle(tls, &tls_e);
        /* Try to get TLS stack level error and certificate failure flags, if any */
        ret = esp_tls_get_and_clear_last_error(tls_e, &esp_tls_code, &esp_tls_flags);
        if (ret == ESP_OK) {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "TLS error = -0x%x, TLS flags = -0x%x", esp_tls_code, esp_tls_flags);
        }
        goto cleanup;
    }

    size_t written_bytes = 0;
    do {
        ret = esp_tls_conn_write(tls,
                                 REQUEST + written_bytes,
                                 strlen(REQUEST) - written_bytes);
        if (ret >= 0) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "%d bytes written", ret);
            written_bytes += ret;
        } else if (ret != ESP_TLS_ERR_SSL_WANT_READ  && ret != ESP_TLS_ERR_SSL_WANT_WRITE) {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "esp_tls_conn_write  returned: [0x%02X](%s)", ret, esp_err_to_name(ret));
            goto cleanup;
        }
    } while (written_bytes < strlen(REQUEST));

    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Reading HTTP response...");
    do {
        len = sizeof(buf) - 1;
        memset(buf, 0x00, sizeof(buf));
        ret = esp_tls_conn_read(tls, (char *)buf, len);

        if (ret == ESP_TLS_ERR_SSL_WANT_WRITE  || ret == ESP_TLS_ERR_SSL_WANT_READ) {
            continue;
        } else if (ret < 0) {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "esp_tls_conn_read  returned [-0x%02X](%s)", -ret, esp_err_to_name(ret));
            break;
        } else if (ret == 0) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "connection closed");
            break;
        }

        len = ret;
        ESP_LOGD(TAG, "%d bytes read", len);
        /* Print response directly to stdout as it is read */
        for (int i = 0; i < len; i++) {
            putchar(buf[i]);
        }
        putchar('\n'); // JSON output doesn't have a newline at end
    } while (1);

#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_CLIENT_SESSION_TICKETS
    /* The TLS session is successfully established, now saving the session ctx for reuse */
    if (save_client_session) {
        esp_tls_free_client_session(tls_client_session);
        tls_client_session = esp_tls_get_client_session(tls);
    }
#endif

cleanup:
    esp_tls_conn_destroy(tls);
exit:
    for (int countdown = 10; countdown >= 0; countdown--) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "%d...", countdown);
        vTaskDelay(1000 / portTICK_PERIOD_MS);
    }
}

#if CONFIG_MBEDTLS_CERTIFICATE_BUNDLE && CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS
static void https_get_request_using_crt_bundle(void)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request using crt bundle");
    esp_tls_cfg_t cfg = {
        .crt_bundle_attach = esp_crt_bundle_attach,
    };
    https_get_request(cfg, WEB_URL, HOWSMYSSL_REQUEST);
}
#endif // CONFIG_MBEDTLS_CERTIFICATE_BUNDLE && CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS

static void https_get_request_using_cacert_buf(void)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request using cacert_buf");
    esp_tls_cfg_t cfg = {
        .cacert_buf = (const unsigned char *) server_root_cert_pem_start,
        .cacert_bytes = server_root_cert_pem_end - server_root_cert_pem_start,
    };
    https_get_request(cfg, WEB_URL, HOWSMYSSL_REQUEST);
}

static void https_get_request_using_specified_ciphersuites(void)
{
#if CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS

    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request using server supported ciphersuites");
    esp_tls_cfg_t cfg = {
        .cacert_buf = (const unsigned char *) server_root_cert_pem_start,
        .cacert_bytes = server_root_cert_pem_end - server_root_cert_pem_start,
        .ciphersuites_list = server_supported_ciphersuites,
    };

    https_get_request(cfg, WEB_URL, HOWSMYSSL_REQUEST);

    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request using server unsupported ciphersuites");

    cfg.ciphersuites_list = server_unsupported_ciphersuites;

    https_get_request(cfg, WEB_URL, HOWSMYSSL_REQUEST);
#endif
}

static void https_get_request_using_global_ca_store(void)
{
    esp_err_t esp_ret = ESP_FAIL;
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request using global ca_store");
    esp_ret = esp_tls_set_global_ca_store(server_root_cert_pem_start, server_root_cert_pem_end - server_root_cert_pem_start);
    if (esp_ret != ESP_OK) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Error in setting the global ca store: [%02X] (%s),could not complete the https_request using global_ca_store", esp_ret, esp_err_to_name(esp_ret));
        return;
    }
    esp_tls_cfg_t cfg = {
        .use_global_ca_store = true,
    };
    https_get_request(cfg, WEB_URL, HOWSMYSSL_REQUEST);
    esp_tls_free_global_ca_store();
}

#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_CLIENT_SESSION_TICKETS
static void https_get_request_to_local_server(const char* url)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request to local server");
    esp_tls_cfg_t cfg = {
        .cacert_buf = (const unsigned char *) local_server_cert_pem_start,
        .cacert_bytes = local_server_cert_pem_end - local_server_cert_pem_start,
        .skip_common_name = true,
    };
    save_client_session = true;
    https_get_request(cfg, url, LOCAL_SRV_REQUEST);
}

static void https_get_request_using_already_saved_session(const char *url)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "https_request using saved client session");
    esp_tls_cfg_t cfg = {
        .client_session = tls_client_session,
        .cacert_buf = (const unsigned char *) local_server_cert_pem_start,
        .cacert_bytes = local_server_cert_pem_end - local_server_cert_pem_start,
        .skip_common_name = true,
    };
    https_get_request(cfg, url, LOCAL_SRV_REQUEST);
    esp_tls_free_client_session(tls_client_session);
    save_client_session = false;
    tls_client_session = NULL;
}
#endif

static void https_request_task(void *pvparameters)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Start https_request example");

#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_CLIENT_SESSION_TICKETS
    char *server_url = NULL;
#ifdef CONFIG_EXAMPLE_LOCAL_SERVER_URL_FROM_STDIN
    char url_buf[SERVER_URL_MAX_SZ];
    if (strcmp(CONFIG_EXAMPLE_LOCAL_SERVER_URL, "FROM_STDIN") == 0) {
        example_configure_stdin_stdout();
        fgets(url_buf, SERVER_URL_MAX_SZ, stdin);
        int len = strlen(url_buf);
        url_buf[len - 1] = '\0';
        server_url = url_buf;
    } else {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Configuration mismatch: invalid url for local server");
        abort();
    }
    printf("\nServer URL obtained is %s\n", url_buf);
#else
    server_url = CONFIG_EXAMPLE_LOCAL_SERVER_URL;
#endif /* CONFIG_EXAMPLE_LOCAL_SERVER_URL_FROM_STDIN */
    https_get_request_to_local_server(server_url);
    https_get_request_using_already_saved_session(server_url);
#endif

#if CONFIG_MBEDTLS_CERTIFICATE_BUNDLE && CONFIG_EXAMPLE_USING_ESP_TLS_MBEDTLS
    https_get_request_using_crt_bundle();
#endif
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Minimum free heap size: %" PRIu32 " bytes", esp_get_minimum_free_heap_size());
    https_get_request_using_cacert_buf();
    https_get_request_using_global_ca_store();
    https_get_request_using_specified_ciphersuites();
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Finish https_request example");
    vTaskDelete(NULL);
}

// ===== main/idf_component.yml =====
dependencies:

// ===== main/time_sync.c =====
/*
 * SPDX-FileCopyrightText: 2021-2022 Espressif Systems (Shanghai) CO LTD
 *
 * SPDX-License-Identifier: Apache-2.0
 */
#include <string.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <time.h>
#include <sys/time.h>
#include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
#include "freertos/task.h"
#include "freertos/event_groups.h"
#include "esp_wifi.h"
#include "esp_event.h"
#include "esp_log.h"
#include "esp_system.h"
#include "nvs_flash.h"
#include "nvs.h"
#include "esp_netif.h"
#include "esp_netif_sntp.h"

#include "lwip/err.h"
#include "lwip/sockets.h"
#include "lwip/sys.h"
#include "lwip/netdb.h"
#include "lwip/dns.h"
#include "time_sync.h"

static const char *TAG = "time_sync";

#define STORAGE_NAMESPACE "storage"

void initialize_sntp(void)
{
    ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Initializing SNTP");
    esp_sntp_config_t config = ESP_NETIF_SNTP_DEFAULT_CONFIG_MULTIPLE(2,
                               ESP_SNTP_SERVER_LIST("time.windows.com", "pool.ntp.org" ) );
    esp_netif_sntp_init(&config);
}

static esp_err_t obtain_time(void)
{
    // wait for time to be set
    int retry = 0;
    const int retry_count = 10;
    while (esp_netif_sntp_sync_wait(pdMS_TO_TICKS(2000)) != ESP_OK && ++retry < retry_count) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Waiting for system time to be set... (%d/%d)", retry, retry_count);
    }
    if (retry == retry_count) {
        return ESP_FAIL;
    }
    return ESP_OK;
}

esp_err_t fetch_and_store_time_in_nvs(void *args)
{
    nvs_handle_t my_handle = 0;
    esp_err_t err;

    initialize_sntp();
    if (obtain_time() != ESP_OK) {
        err = ESP_FAIL;
        goto exit;
    }

    time_t now;
    time(&now);

    //Open
    err = nvs_open(STORAGE_NAMESPACE, NVS_READWRITE, &my_handle);
    if (err != ESP_OK) {
        goto exit;
    }

    //Write
    err = nvs_set_i64(my_handle, "timestamp", now);
    if (err != ESP_OK) {
        goto exit;
    }

    err = nvs_commit(my_handle);
    if (err != ESP_OK) {
        goto exit;
    }

exit:
    if (my_handle != 0) {
        nvs_close(my_handle);
    }
    esp_netif_sntp_deinit();

    if (err != ESP_OK) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Error updating time in nvs");
    } else {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Updated time in NVS");
    }
    return err;
}

esp_err_t update_time_from_nvs(void)
{
    nvs_handle_t my_handle = 0;
    esp_err_t err;

    err = nvs_open(STORAGE_NAMESPACE, NVS_READWRITE, &my_handle);
    if (err != ESP_OK) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Error opening NVS");
        goto exit;
    }

    int64_t timestamp = 0;

    err = nvs_get_i64(my_handle, "timestamp", &timestamp);
    if (err == ESP_ERR_NVS_NOT_FOUND) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Time not found in NVS. Syncing time from SNTP server.");
        if (fetch_and_store_time_in_nvs(NULL) != ESP_OK) {
            err = ESP_FAIL;
        } else {
            err = ESP_OK;
        }
    } else if (err == ESP_OK) {
        struct timeval get_nvs_time;
        get_nvs_time.tv_sec = timestamp;
        settimeofday(&get_nvs_time, NULL);
    }

exit:
    if (my_handle != 0) {
        nvs_close(my_handle);
    }
    return err;
}

   36      HTTP_EVENT_ERROR = 0,       /*!< This event occurs when there are any errors during execution */
   37      HTTP_EVENT_ON_CONNECTED,    /*!< Once the HTTP has been connected to the server, no data exchange has been performed */
   38      HTTP_EVENT_HEADERS_SENT,     /*!< After sending all the headers to the server */
   39      HTTP_EVENT_HEADER_SENT = HTTP_EVENT_HEADERS_SENT, /*!< This header has been kept for backward compatibility
   40                                                             and will be deprecated in future versions esp-idf */
   41      HTTP_EVENT_ON_HEADER,       /*!< Occurs when receiving each header sent from the server */
   42      HTTP_EVENT_ON_DATA,         /*!< Occurs when receiving data from the server, possibly multiple portions of the packet */
   43      HTTP_EVENT_ON_FINISH,       /*!< Occurs when finish a HTTP session */
   44      HTTP_EVENT_DISCONNECTED,    /*!< The connection has been disconnected */

   32      int intr_priority;                    /*!< I2C interrupt priority, if set to 0, driver will select the default priority (1,2,3). */
   33      size_t trans_queue_depth;             /*!< Depth of internal transfer queue, increase this value can support more transfers pending in the background, only valid in asynchronous transaction. (Typically max_device_num * per_transaction)*/
   34      struct {

   38                                                This can save power, but at the expense of more RAM being consumed */

{
  "path": "C:\\Espressif\\projects\\my_firmware\\assistive_wearable\\firmware\\platforms\\esp32\\mpu6050.c",
  "content": "#include \"mpu6050.h\"\n#include \"app_config.h\"\n#include \"logger.h\"\n#include <math.h>\n#include <string.h>\n#include \"freertos/FreeRTOS.h\"\n#include \"freertos/task.h\"\n#include \"esp_timer.h\"\n\nstatic const char *TAG = \"mpu6050\";\nstatic i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus;\nstatic i2c_master_dev_handle_t dev;\nstatic float gyro_bias[3];\nstatic float gravity_mag = 1.0f;\nstatic float gravity_lpf[3] = {0.0f, 0.0f, 1.0f};\nstatic float roll_deg;\nstatic float pitch_deg;\n\nstatic esp_err_t reg_read(uint8_t reg, uint8_t *data, size_t len)\n{\n    return i2c_master_transmit_receive(dev, &reg, 1, data, len, pdMS_TO_TICKS(50));\n}\n\nstatic esp_err_t reg_write(uint8_t reg, uint8_t value)\n{\n    uint8_t buf[2] = {reg, value};\n    return i2c_master_transmit(dev, buf, sizeof(buf), pdMS_TO_TICKS(50));\n}\n\nesp_err_t mpu6050_init(void)\n{\n    i2c_master_bus_config_t bus_cfg = {\n        .i2c_port = I2C_NUM_0,\n        .sda_io_num = APP_I2C_SDA_GPIO,\n        .scl_io_num = APP_I2C_SCL_GPIO,\n        .clk_source = I2C_CLK_SRC_DEFAULT,\n        .glitch_ignore_cnt = 7,\n        .flags.enable_internal_pullup = true,\n    };\n    esp_err_t err = i2c_new_master_bus(&bus_cfg, &bus);\n    if (err != ESP_OK) return err;\n\n    i2c_device_config_t dev_cfg = {\n        .dev_addr_length = I2C_ADDR_BIT_LEN_7,\n        .device_address = APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS,\n        .scl_speed_hz = APP_I2C_FREQUENCY_HZ,\n    };\n    err = i2c_master_bus_add_device(bus, &dev_cfg, &dev);\n    if (err != ESP_OK) return err;\n\n    uint8_t who = 0;\n    err = reg_read(0x75, &who, 1);\n    if (err != ESP_OK || (who != 0x68 && who != 0x69)) {\n        ESP_LOGE(TAG, \"MPU6050 WHO_AM_I failed: 0x%02x\", who);\n        return err == ESP_OK ? ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND : err;\n    }\n    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x6B, 0x00));\n    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x1C, 0x10)); /* +/-8 g */\n    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x1B, 0x10)); /* +/-1000 dps */\n    ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x1A, 0x03)); /* digital low-pass filter */\n    ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"MPU6050 ready at 0x%02x\", APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS);\n    return ESP_OK;\n}\n\nesp_err_t mpu6050_calibrate(uint32_t seconds)\n{\n    const uint32_t count = seconds * APP_SAMPLE_HZ;\n    double sum_g[3] = {0};\n    double sum_a[3] = {0};\n    uint8_t raw[14];\n    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < count; ++i) {\n        esp_err_t err = reg_read(0x3B, raw, sizeof(raw));\n        if (err != ESP_OK) return err;\n        for (int axis = 0; axis < 3; ++axis) {\n            int16_t av = (int16_t)((raw[axis * 2] << 8) | raw[axis * 2 + 1]);\n            int16_t gv = (int16_t)((raw[8 + axis * 2] << 8) | raw[9 + axis * 2]);\n            sum_a[axis] += (float)av / 4096.0f;\n            sum_g[axis] += (float)gv / 32.8f;\n        }\n        vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000 / APP_SAMPLE_HZ));\n    }\n    for (int axis = 0; axis < 3; ++axis) {\n        gyro_bias[axis] = (float)(sum_g[axis] / count);\n        gravity_lpf[axis] = (float)(sum_a[axis] / count);\n    }\n    gravity_mag = sqrtf(gravity_lpf[0] * gravity_lpf[0] + gravity_lpf[1] * gravity_lpf[1] + gravity_lpf[2] * gravity_lpf[2]);\n    ESP_LOGI(TAG, \"calibration complete: gyro bias %.2f %.2f %.2f dps\", gyro_bias[0], gyro_bias[1], gyro_bias[2]);\n    return ESP_OK;\n}\n\nesp_err_t mpu6050_read(mpu6050_sample_t *sample)\n{\n    if (!sample) return ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG;\n    uint8_t raw[14];\n    esp_err_t err = reg_read(0x3B, raw, sizeof(raw));\n    if (err != ESP_OK) return err;\n    int16_t v[7];\n    for (int i = 0; i < 7; ++i) v[i] = (int16_t)((raw[i * 2] << 8) | raw[i * 2 + 1]);\n    sample->ax_g = v[0] / 4096.0f;\n    sample->ay_g = v[1] / 4096.0f;\n    sample->az_g = v[2] / 4096.0f;\n    sample->gx_dps = v[4] / 32.8f - gyro_bias[0];\n    sample->gy_dps = v[5] / 32.8f - gyro_bias[1];\n    sample->gz_dps = v[6] / 32.8f - gyro_bias[2];\n    sample->accel_mag_g = sqrtf(sample->ax_g * sample->ax_g + sample->ay_g * sample->ay_g + sample->az_g * sample->az_g);\n    sample->gyro_mag_dps = sqrtf(sample->gx_dps * sample->gx_dps + sample->gy_dps * sample->gy_dps + sample->gz_dps * sample->gz_dps);\n    const float alpha = 0.08f;\n    gravity_lpf[0] += alpha * (sample->ax_g - gravity_lpf[0]);\n    gravity_lpf[1] += alpha * (sample->ay_g - gravity_lpf[1]);\n    gravity_lpf[2] += alpha * (sample->az_g - gravity_lpf[2]);\n    sample->dynamic_accel_g = fabsf(sample->accel_mag_g - gravity_mag);\n    sample->roll_deg = atan2f(gravity_lpf[1], gravity_lpf[2]) * 57.29578f;\n    sample->pitch_deg = atan2f(-gravity_lpf[0], sqrtf(gravity_lpf[1] * gravity_lpf[1] + gravity_lpf[2] * gravity_lpf[2])) * 57.29578f;\n    roll_deg = sample->roll_deg;\n    pitch_deg = sample->pitch_deg;\n    return ESP_OK;\n}\n"
}

+  };
+  esp_err_t err = i2c_new_master_bus(&bus_cfg, &bus);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) return err;
+  i2c_device_config_t dev_cfg = {
+  .dev_addr_length = I2C_ADDR_BIT_LEN_7,
+  .device_address = APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS,
+  .scl_speed_hz = APP_I2C_FREQUENCY_HZ,
+  };
+  err = i2c_master_bus_add_device(bus, &dev_cfg, &dev);
+  if (err != ESP_OK) return err;
+  uint8_t who = 0;
+  err = reg_read(0x75, &who, 1);
+  if (err != ESP_OK || (who != 0x68 && who != 0x69)) {
+  ESP_LOGE(TAG, "MPU6050 WHO_AM_I failed: 0x%02x", who);
+  return err == ESP_OK ? ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND : err;
+  }
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x6B, 0x00));
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x1C, 0x10)); /* +/-8 g */
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x1B, 0x10)); /* +/-1000 dps */
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(reg_write(0x1A, 0x03)); /* digital low-pass filter */
+  ESP_LOGI(TAG, "MPU6050 ready at 0x%02x", APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS);
+  return ESP_OK;
+ }
+ esp_err_t mpu6050_calibrate(uint32_t seconds)
+ {
+  const uint32_t count = seconds * APP_SAMPLE_HZ;
+  double sum_g[3] = {0};
+  double sum_a[3] = {0};
+  uint8_t raw[14];
+  for (uint32_t i = 0; i < count; ++i) {
+  esp_err_t err = reg_read(0x3B, raw, sizeof(raw));
+  if (err != ESP_OK) return err;
+  for (int axis = 0; axis < 3; ++axis) {
+  int16_t av = (int16_t)((raw[axis * 2] << 8) | raw[axis * 2 + 1]);
+  int16_t gv = (int16_t)((raw[8 + axis * 2] << 8) | raw[9 + axis * 2]);
+  sum_a[axis] += (float)av / 4096.0f;
+  sum_g[axis] += (float)gv / 32.8f;
+  }
+  vTaskDelay(pdMS_TO_TICKS(1000 / APP_SAMPLE_HZ));
+  }
+  for (int axis = 0; axis < 3; ++axis) {
+  gyro_bias[axis] = (float)(sum_g[axis] / count);
+  gravity_lpf[axis] = (float)(sum_a[axis] / count);
+  }
+  gravity_mag = sqrtf(gravity_lpf[0] * gravity_lpf[0] + gravity_lpf[1] * gravity_lpf[1] + gravity_lpf[2] * gravity_lpf[2]);
+  ESP_LOGI(TAG, "calibration complete: gyro bias %.2f %.2f %.2f dps", gyro_bias[0], gyro_bias[1], gyro_bias[2]);
+  return ESP_OK;
+ }
+ esp_err_t mpu6050_read(mpu6050_sample_t *sample)
+ {
+  if (!sample) return ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG;
+  uint8_t raw[14];
+  esp_err_t err = reg_read(0x3B, raw, sizeof(raw));
+  if (err != ESP_OK) return err;
+  int16_t v[7];
+  for (int i = 0; i < 7; ++i) v[i] = (int16_t)((raw[i * 2] << 8) | raw[i * 2 + 1]);
+  sample->ax_g = v[0] / 4096.0f;
+  sample->ay_g = v[1] / 4096.0f;
+  sample->az_g = v[2] / 4096.0f;
+  sample->gx_dps = v[4] / 32.8f - gyro_bias[0];
+  sample->gy_dps = v[5] / 32.8f - gyro_bias[1];
+  sample->gz_dps = v[6] / 32.8f - gyro_bias[2];
+  sample->accel_mag_g = sqrtf(sample->ax_g * sample->ax_g + sample->ay_g * sample->ay_g + sample->az_g * sample->az_g);
+  sample->gyro_mag_dps = sqrtf(sample->gx_dps * sample->gx_dps + sample->gy_dps * sample->gy_dps + sample->gz_dps * sample->gz_dps);
+  const float alpha = 0.08f;
+  gravity_lpf[0] += alpha * (sample->ax_g - gravity_lpf[0]);
+  gravity_lpf[1] += alpha * (sample->ay_g - gravity_lpf[1]);
+  gravity_lpf[2] += alpha * (sample->az_g - gravity_lpf[2]);
+  sample->dynamic_accel_g = fabsf(sample->accel_mag_g - gravity_mag);
+  sample->roll_deg = atan2f(gravity_lpf[1], gravity_lpf[2]) * 57.29578f;
+  sample->pitch_deg = atan2f(-gravity_lpf[0], sqrtf(gravity_lpf[1] * gravity_lpf[1] + gravity_lpf[2] * gravity_lpf[2])) * 57.29578f;
+  roll_deg = sample->roll_deg;
+  pitch_deg = sample->pitch_deg;
+  return ESP_OK;
+ }

+ #include "blynk_client.h"
+ #include "app_config.h"
+ #include "logger.h"
+ #include <stdio.h>
+ #include <string.h>
+ #include "esp_wifi.h"
+ #include "esp_event.h"
+ #include "esp_netif.h"
+ #include "nvs_flash.h"
+ #include "esp_http_client.h"
+ #include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
+ #include "freertos/event_groups.h"
+ static const char *TAG = "blynk";
+ static EventGroupHandle_t wifi_events;
+ static bool connected;
+ #define WIFI_BIT BIT0
+ static void wifi_handler(void *arg, esp_event_base_t base, int32_t id, void *data)
+ {
+  if (base == WIFI_EVENT && id == WIFI_EVENT_STA_START) esp_wifi_connect();
+  if (base == WIFI_EVENT && id == WIFI_EVENT_STA_DISCONNECTED) { connected = false; esp_wifi_connect(); }
+  if (base == IP_EVENT && id == IP_EVENT_STA_GOT_IP) { connected = true; xEventGroupSetBits(wifi_events, WIFI_BIT); ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Wi-Fi connected"); }
+ }
+ void blynk_init(void)
+ {
+  wifi_events = xEventGroupCreate();
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_netif_init());
+  esp_err_t event_err = esp_event_loop_create_default();
+  if (event_err != ESP_OK && event_err != ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE) ESP_ERROR_CHECK(event_err);
+  esp_netif_create_default_wifi_sta();
+  wifi_init_config_t init = WIFI_INIT_CONFIG_DEFAULT();
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_init(&init));
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_event_handler_register(WIFI_EVENT, ESP_EVENT_ANY_ID, wifi_handler, NULL));
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_event_handler_register(IP_EVENT, IP_EVENT_STA_GOT_IP, wifi_handler, NULL));
+  wifi_config_t config = { .sta = { .ssid = APP_WIFI_SSID, .password = APP_WIFI_PASSWORD, .threshold.authmode = WIFI_AUTH_WPA2_PSK } };
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_set_mode(WIFI_MODE_STA));
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_set_config(WIFI_IF_STA, &config));
+  ESP_ERROR_CHECK(esp_wifi_start());
+ }
+ void blynk_publish(const imu_sample_t *s, const recognition_result_t *r)
+ {
+  if (!connected || !s || !r || strcmp(APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, "YOUR_BLYNK_DEVICE_TOKEN") == 0) return;
+  char url[384];
+  snprintf(url, sizeof(url), "%s/external/api/batch/update?token=%s&v0=%.3f&v1=%.3f&v2=%.3f&v3=%.2f&v4=%.2f&v5=%.2f&v6=%s&v7=%s&v8=%s", APP_BLYNK_HOST, APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, s->ax_g, s->ay_g, s->az_g, s->gx_dps, s->gy_dps, s->gz_dps, activity_name(r->activity), r->gesture, r->fall_confirmed ? "FALL_DETECTED" : "Normal");
+  esp_http_client_config_t cfg = { .url = url, .method = HTTP_METHOD_GET, .timeout_ms = 3000 };
+  esp_http_client_handle_t client = esp_http_client_init(&cfg);
+  if (client) { esp_err_t err = esp_http_client_perform(client); if (err != ESP_OK) ESP_LOGW(TAG, "publish failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err)); esp_http_client_cleanup(client); }
+ }

🔧 create_textdoc 
{
  "path": "C:\\Espressif\\projects\\my_firmware\\assistive_wearable\\firmware\\README.md",
  "content": "# Assistive Wearable\n\nAn ESP32-C3 + MPU6050-only assistive wearable prototype. It recognizes deliberate tilt/shake gestures, estimates basic activity, detects possible falls using a multi-condition state machine, and publishes status to Blynk over Wi-Fi. The smartphone is only the communication, accessibility, notification, and feedback layer; no additional physical sensors are used.\n\n## Hardware and wiring\n\n| MPU6050 | ESP32-C3 |\n|---|---|\n| VCC | 3.3 V |\n| GND | GND |\n| SDA | GPIO4 |\n| SCL | GPIO5 |\n\nUse a breakout with I2C pull-ups, or add only the breakout's normal pull-ups as part of the MPU6050 module. The prototype uses address 0x68; set `APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS` to 0x69 if AD0 is high.\n\n## Firmware behavior\n\nThe sensor is sampled at 100 Hz. The MPU6050 is configured for ±8 g, ±1000 dps, and its digital low-pass filter. A five-second stationary startup calibration estimates gyro bias and the gravity vector. The driver combines low-pass gravity, dynamic acceleration, jerk, gyro magnitude, and roll/pitch.\n\nGestures require angular motion and a tilt, then apply a cooldown. Right/left map to Next/Previous, forward/backward map to Confirm/Back, and shakes map to Repeat or Emergency when two occur close together. The initial activity classifier is intentionally rule-based and classifies Idle, Standing, Walking, Running, and Sitting heuristically; mounting position must remain consistent.\n\nFall detection requires a high dynamic-acceleration change, high gyro activity, jerk, a posture change, and post-event inactivity. A candidate is not reported as confirmed until the state persists and is not cancelled by resumed motion. The current confirmation window is eight seconds. This is a research prototype, not a certified medical or emergency-response device; never validate falls using dangerous intentional drops of a person.\n\n## Blynk setup\n\nSet placeholders in `firmware/configs/app_config.h` locally: `APP_WIFI_SSID`, `APP_WIFI_PASSWORD`, and `APP_BLYNK_TOKEN`. Do not commit real credentials. Create Blynk virtual datastreams V0–V8 for accelerometer X/Y/Z, gyro X/Y/Z, activity, gesture, and fall status. Configure Blynk events for `fall_detected` and `emergency_gesture`, with concise notifications. Use Android TalkBack or iOS VoiceOver and enable notification read-aloud/accessibility features; a dashboard alone is not sufficient for a visually impaired user.\n\nThe firmware remains locally functional when Wi-Fi is unavailable. Blynk telemetry is rate-limited to approximately 1 Hz, while local status changes are logged immediately.\n\n## Build and test\n\nBuild with ESP-IDF 5.5 for `esp32c3`. On boot, keep the wearable still through calibration and verify the WHO_AM_I log. Test gestures while seated and while walking, then measure false positives across normal walking, sitting, running, and intentional motion. Tune thresholds from labeled data at the actual body mounting location. Test Wi-Fi loss and MPU6050 disconnect behavior before enabling notifications for users.\n\nRule-based detection is the initial choice because it is explainable and resource efficient. A later TinyML model should use labeled MPU6050-only windows and replace individual classifiers only if measured accuracy justifies the additional complexity.\n"
}

It recognizes deliberate tilt/shake gestures, estimates basic activity, detects possible falls using a multi-condition state machine, and publishes status to Blynk over Wi-Fi. 📄 edit: \\?\C:\Espressif\projects\my_firmware\assistive_wearable\firmware\README.md (L9–16)
- - `app/` — application entry/orchestration (`app_start()` in `app.c`); includes service headers only.
- - `interfaces/` — vendor-neutral contracts (e.g. `*_if.h`) that platforms implement.
- - `platforms/` — vendor/platform-specific implementations; ESP-IDF (`esp_*`, `driver/*`) code lives here.
- - `services/`, `modules/` — vendor-agnostic application logic.
- - `configs/` — compile-time config (`app_config.h` for pins & constants).
- - `utils/` — logging and helpers.
- - `tests/`, `scripts/`, `docs/` — supporting material (expand these as the project grows).

+ |---|---|

📄 edit: \\?\C:\Espressif\projects\my_firmware\assistive_wearable\firmware\README.md (L17–20)
- The ESP-IDF entry point `app_main()` lives once in `../main/entry.c` and only
- calls `app_start()`. The `main/` component is generated build glue — edit the
- `firmware/` tree, not `main/`.
+ Use a breakout with I2C pull-ups, or add only the breakout's normal pull-ups as part of the MPU6050 module. The prototype uses address 0x68; set `APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS` to 0x69 if AD0 is high.
+ ## Firmware behavior
+ The sensor is sampled at 100 Hz. The MPU6050 is configured for ±8 g, ±1000 dps, and its digital low-pass filter. A five-second stationary startup calibration estimates gyro bias and the gravity vector. The driver combines low-pass gravity, dynamic acceleration, jerk, gyro magnitude, and roll/pitch.
+ Gestures require angular motion and a tilt, then apply a cooldown. Right/left map to Next/Previous, forward/backward map to Confirm/Back, and shakes map to Repeat or Emergency when two occur close together. The initial activity classifier is intentionally rule-based and classifies Idle, Standing, Walking, Running, and Sitting heuristically; mounting position must remain consistent.
+ Fall detection requires a high dynamic-acceleration change, high gyro activity, jerk, a posture change, and post-event inactivity. A candidate is not reported as confirmed until the state persists and is not cancelled by resumed motion. The current confirmation window is eight seconds. This is a research prototype, not a certified medical or emergency-response device; never validate falls using dangerous intentional drops of a person.
+ ## Blynk setup
+ Set placeholders in `firmware/configs/app_config.h` locally: `APP_WIFI_SSID`, `APP_WIFI_PASSWORD`, and `APP_BLYNK_TOKEN`. Do not commit real credentials. Create Blynk virtual datastreams V0–V8 for accelerometer X/Y/Z, gyro X/Y/Z, activity, gesture, and fall status. Configure Blynk events for `fall_detected` and `emergency_gesture`, with concise notifications. Use Android TalkBack or iOS VoiceOver and enable notification read-aloud/accessibility features; a dashboard alone is not sufficient for a visually impaired user.
+ The firmware remains locally functional when Wi-Fi is unavailable. Blynk telemetry is rate-limited to approximately 1 Hz, while local status changes are logged immediately.
+ ## Build and test
+ Build with ESP-IDF 5.5 for `esp32c3`. On boot, keep the wearable still through calibration and verify the WHO_AM_I log. Test gestures while seated and while walking, then measure false positives across normal walking, sitting, running, and intentional motion. Tune thresholds from labeled data at the actual body mounting location. Test Wi-Fi loss and MPU6050 disconnect behavior before enabling notifications for users.
+ Rule-based detection is the initial choice because it is explainable and resource efficient. A later TinyML model should use labeled MPU6050-only windows and replace individual classifiers only if measured accuracy justifies the additional complexity.

#define BLYNK_TEMPLATE_ID "TMPL6H7bh4Ptg"

ssid : Hotspot
passoord: security

I want to build a complete working prototype called:

**“Smart Assistive Wearable for Visually Impaired People Using ESP32, MPU6050 and Blynk IoT”**

### HARDWARE RESTRICTION

Use ONLY:

1. ESP32
2. MPU6050

The MPU6050 must be the **only physical sensor**.

Do NOT add:

* Ultrasonic sensor
* ToF sensor
* GPS
* GSM
* Camera
* Additional IMU
* Heart-rate sensor
* Any other sensor

The smartphone and Blynk IoT are allowed only for communication, notifications, user interface, and feedback.

---

# MAIN OBJECTIVE

Create a wearable system for a visually impaired person that can:

1. Detect intentional hand gestures using MPU6050.
2. Detect falls using MPU6050.
3. Send the detected events to Blynk IoT.
4. Trigger a Blynk phone notification/alarm when a fall is detected.
5. Ask the user whether they are OK.
6. Allow the user to respond using MPU6050 gestures.
7. If the user says OK, cancel the emergency state.
8. If the user indicates NOT OK, trigger a higher-priority emergency event.
9. If the user does not respond within a configurable time, trigger an emergency event.
10. Display activity, gesture, fall status and event history in Blynk.

---

# GESTURE SYSTEM

Use simple gestures that a visually impaired person can perform without looking at the device.

Initially implement:

### Gesture 1 — LEFT

User moves/tilts the wearable toward the left.

Meaning:

**“I AM OK”**

When a fall alert is active:

* Send `USER_OK` to ESP32 logic.
* Cancel the local fall alarm/state.
* Send a Blynk event indicating that the user responded OK.

### Gesture 2 — RIGHT

User moves/tilts the wearable toward the right.

Meaning:

**“I NEED HELP”**

When a fall alert is active:

* Send `USER_NEEDS_HELP`.
* Keep the emergency state active.
* Trigger a high-priority Blynk event/notification.

### Gesture 3 — DOUBLE SHAKE

Two deliberate shakes within a short configurable time.

Meaning:

**“EMERGENCY”**

This should trigger an immediate Blynk emergency event even if no fall occurred.

### Gesture 4 — FORWARD

Forward tilt/movement.

Meaning:

**SELECT / CONFIRM**

### Gesture 5 — BACKWARD

Backward tilt/movement.

Meaning:

**CANCEL / BACK**

Make all gesture thresholds configurable.

---

# FALL DETECTION

Do NOT detect a fall simply by checking whether acceleration exceeds one threshold.

Use the MPU6050 accelerometer and gyroscope together.

The fall algorithm should consider:

* Total acceleration magnitude
* Sudden acceleration/impact
* Gyroscope magnitude
* Sudden orientation change
* Post-impact inactivity
* Time duration of the event

Suggested logic:

```text
Normal movement
↓
Sudden acceleration/impact
↓
Orientation change
↓
Post-impact inactivity
↓
Possible fall
↓
Confirmation period
↓
Fall confirmed
```

The algorithm should try to distinguish:

* Walking
* Standing
* Sitting
* Normal hand movement
* Intentional gestures
* Sudden fall

from an actual fall.

Explain every threshold and make the thresholds configurable in the code.

---

# FALL RESPONSE WORKFLOW

When a fall is confirmed:

```text
MPU6050
↓
ESP32 detects fall
↓
FALL_CONFIRMED
↓
Blynk Fall Event
↓
Phone notification/alarm
↓
“Possible fall detected. Are you OK?”
↓
Wait for user response
↓
┌───────────────────────┐
│ │
LEFT RIGHT
│ │
↓ ↓
I AM OK NEED HELP
│ │
↓ ↓
Cancel alert Emergency
notification
```

Also implement a timeout.

For example:

```text
Fall detected
↓
15-second response window
↓
No response
↓
EMERGENCY_NO_RESPONSE
```

Make the timeout configurable.

# BLYNK IoT

Use the current Blynk IoT platform.

ESP32 should connect to Blynk using Wi-Fi.

Architecture:

```text
MPU6050
↓ I2C
ESP32
↓ Wi-Fi
Blynk Cloud
↓
Blynk Mobile App
```

Use Blynk Virtual Pins/Datastreams for the project.

Suggested Datastreams:

```text
V0 = Current Activity
V1 = Last Gesture
V2 = Fall Status
V3 = Accelerometer X
V4 = Accelerometer Y
V5 = Accelerometer Z
V6 = Gyroscope X
V7 = Gyroscope Y
V8 = Gyroscope Z
V9 = Tilt Angle
V10 = System State
V11 = Fall Response
V12 = Device Status
```

Use suitable data types for each.

Do not continuously send raw MPU6050 data at an unnecessarily high rate. Perform sensor processing locally on the ESP32 and send useful/processed data to Blynk at a reasonable interval.

# CALIBRATION

Create a startup calibration procedure.

The device should:

1. Ask the user to keep the device still.

3. Calculate offsets/baseline values.
4. Store or use the calibration values.
5. Start monitoring.

Explain how accelerometer and gyroscope offsets affect the system.

# FALSE POSITIVE REDUCTION

This is very important.

The system should not interpret every normal movement as a fall.

Test against:

* Walking
* Sitting down
* Standing up
* Running
* Jumping
* Hand gestures
* Shaking the device
* Placing the device on a table
* Dropping the wearable accidentally

Explain how the algorithm distinguishes these cases.

Also ensure that the intentional `LEFT`, `RIGHT`, and `DOUBLE SHAKE` gestures do not accidentally trigger fall detection.

---

# TESTING

Create a detailed test table:

| Test | Expected result |
| -------------------- | --------------------- |
| Normal walking | No fall |
| Left gesture | USER_OK/LEFT |
| Right gesture | USER_NEEDS_HELP/RIGHT |
| Double shake | EMERGENCY |
| Forward gesture | SELECT |
| Backward gesture | CANCEL |
| Simulated fall | FALL_DETECTED |
| Fall + left gesture | USER_OK |
| Fall + right gesture | USER_NEEDS_HELP |
| Fall + no response | NO_RESPONSE |
| Normal sitting | No fall |
| Running | No fall |

---

# IMPORTANT SAFETY REQUIREMENT

This is an engineering prototype and should NOT be described as a medically certified or safety-critical fall detection device.

Clearly state that MPU6050-only fall detection can produce false positives and false negatives and requires extensive testing before any real-world safety use.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Audit the existing ESP-IDF project and preserve the confirmed ESP32-C3 target, GPIO4 SDA/GPIO5 SCL wiring, and MPU6050-only hardware restriction. Keep ESP-IDF rather than switching to Arduino or the Blynk Arduino library.
2. Move runtime settings into configurable ESP-IDF/Kconfig or local configuration values. Use the supplied Blynk template ID/name, Wi-Fi SSID `Hotspot`, password `security`, and a local-only device-token value; do not commit credentials. Default to station-only Wi-Fi, WPA2/WPA3-compatible authentication, bounded exponential reconnect, 100 Hz sampling, 1 Hz telemetry, immediate event delivery, a 3-second fall-confirmation window, and a 15-second fall-response window.
3. Harden the MPU6050 driver with ESP-IDF v5.5 I2C APIs, address probing for 0x68/0x69, WHO_AM_I validation, burst reads, timeout handling, disconnect detection, calibration persistence, timestamped samples, filtering, gravity/dynamic acceleration, jerk, angular velocity, and roll/pitch estimation.
4. Replace the current one-sample recognizer decisions with windowed state machines. Require directional tilt plus angular motion, minimum and maximum gesture duration, return-to-neutral, walking-motion rejection, cooldown, and alternating shake phases before publishing a gesture. Use double-shake detection only for two deliberate shakes inside the configured interval.
5. Implement the full fall and response workflow: NORMAL, IMPACT_SUSPECTED, POST_IMPACT_CHECK, FALL_CONFIRMED_WAITING_RESPONSE, USER_OK, USER_NEEDS_HELP, and EMERGENCY_NO_RESPONSE. Combine acceleration magnitude, impact/jerk, gyroscope magnitude, orientation change, post-impact inactivity, event duration, and activity context; never confirm a fall from acceleration magnitude alone.
6. Add activity recognition over approximately two-second MPU6050-only windows for idle, standing, walking, running, and sitting. Use rule-based features initially because they are explainable and lightweight, while documenting that sitting versus standing depends on consistent mounting orientation and is heuristic.
7. Extend the Blynk HTTPS client with TLS certificate validation, time synchronization, asynchronous/bounded network work, reconnection handling, URL-safe event descriptions, rate limiting, and an offline event queue. Map V0–V13 to the requested activity, gesture, fall, IMU, tilt, state, response, connection, and last-event values.
8. Add Blynk event publishing for `FALL_DETECTED`, `USER_OK`, `USER_NEEDS_HELP`, `EMERGENCY_GESTURE`, and `NO_RESPONSE`. Use concise notification text suitable for phone accessibility features; document that TalkBack, VoiceOver, notification read-aloud, and sound settings are phone responsibilities rather than dashboard functionality.
9. Integrate sensor processing, recognizers, response handling, event deduplication, telemetry, and local serial diagnostics without blocking the 100 Hz sampling loop. Preserve local detection when Wi-Fi or Blynk is unavailable and publish queued critical events after reconnection when they are still valid.
10. Update the README with the complete project concept, architecture, wiring, address selection, calibration, algorithms, configurable thresholds, Blynk Console setup, datastream/widget mapping, event configuration, accessibility instructions, troubleshooting, testing matrix, limitations, and explicit non-medical-prototype safety language.
11. Build with ESP-IDF 5.5, verify that no unavailable managed component or restricted GPIO is referenced, then flash and monitor on the ESP32-C3 with a real MPU6050. Tune thresholds only from controlled labeled trials at the final wearable mounting position; do not validate falls by intentionally dropping a person.
12. Keep the initial implementation rule-based and defer TinyML until measured MPU6050-only data shows that the rule-based classifiers cannot meet the required false-positive and false-negative targets.

**Alternatives:** If the MPU6050 is not detected, run an address scan, verify 3.3 V/GND and the AD0 level, then use the detected 0x68 or 0x69 address without changing GPIO4/GPIO5. If Blynk HTTPS or certificate validation fails, keep local recognition operational, verify SNTP and the ESP-IDF certificate bundle, and isolate cloud publishing in the network task before considering a direct ESP-TLS implementation. If the Blynk external API rejects event or datastream requests, verify the event codes and current Blynk API format and retain the same internal event interface. If rule-based activity or fall detection remains inaccurate after measured tuning, collect labeled MPU6050-only windows and evaluate a small feature-based TinyML model without adding any physical sensor. If Wi-Fi is unavailable, log events locally, retain only bounded recent critical events with expiration, and deliver them after reconnection only when they are still valid.

r->action = \"NONE\"; r->event_code = \"NONE\"; r->activity = ACT_IDLE; r->system_state = SYS_MONITORING;\n    float jerk = fabsf(x->accel_mag_g - s.last_accel); s.last_accel = x->accel_mag_g;\n    s.energy = 0.95f * s.energy + 0.05f * (x->dynamic_accel_g + x->gyro_mag_dps / 180.0f);\n\n    if (!s.candidate && !s.confirmed && x->dynamic_accel_g > APP_FALL_IMPACT_G && x->gyro_mag_dps > APP_FALL_GYRO_DPS && jerk > APP_FALL_JERK_G) {\n        s.candidate = true; s.candidate_at = now; s.active_motion = now; s.max_dynamic = x->dynamic_accel_g;\n    }\n    if (s.candidate || s.confirmed) {\n        if (x->gyro_mag_dps > 35.0f || x->dynamic_accel_g > 0.25f) s.active_motion = now;\n        if (x->dynamic_accel_g > s.max_dynamic) s.max_dynamic = x->dynamic_accel_g;\n        bool posture_change = fabsf(x->roll_deg) > APP_FALL_POSTURE_DEG || fabsf(x->pitch_deg) > APP_FALL_POSTURE_DEG;\n        bool inactive = now - s.active_motion > APP_FALL_INACTIVITY_MS;\n        if (s.candidate && posture_change && inactive && now - s.candidate_at >= APP_FALL_CONFIRM_MS) s.confirmed = true;\n        if (s.candidate && !s.confirmed && now - s.candidate_at > APP_FALL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS) s.candidate = false;\n    }\n    if (s.confirmed && !s.response_reported) {\n        r->activity = ACT_FALL_CONFIRMED; r->system_state = SYS_FALL_WAITING; r->fall_confirmed = true; r->fall_score = 1.0f;\n        set_event(r, \"FALL_CONFIRMED\", \"ARE YOU OK?\", \"FALL_DETECTED\"); s.response_reported = true; return;\n    }\n    if (s.confirmed) {\n        r->activity = ACT_FALL_CONFIRMED; r->system_state = SYS_FALL_WAITING; r->fall_confirmed = true; r->fall_score = 1.0f;\n        if (now - s.candidate_at >= APP_FALL_CONFIRM_MS + APP_FALL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS) {\n            r->system_state = SYS_NO_RESPONSE; set_event(r, \"NO_RESPONSE\", \"EMERGENCY\", \"NO_RESPONSE\"); s.confirmed = false; s.candidate = false; s.response_reported = false;\n        }\n        return;\n    }\n    if (s.candidate) { r->activity = ACT_FALL_CANDIDATE; r->system_state = SYS_FALL_WAITING; r->fall_score = 0.6f; return; }\n\n    if (s.energy < 0.035f) r->activity = ACT_IDLE;\n    else if (s.energy > 0.65f) r->activity = ACT_RUNNING;\n    else if (s.energy > 0.16f) r->activity = ACT_WALKING;\n    else if (fabsf(x->pitch_deg) > 45.0f) r->activity = ACT_SITTING;\n    else r->activity = ACT_STANDING;\n\n    bool cooldown = now - s.last_gesture >= APP_GESTURE_COOLDOWN_MS;\n    bool shake = x->gyro_mag_dps > APP_SHAKE_GYRO_DPS && x->dynamic_accel_g > APP_SHAKE_DYNAMIC_G;\n    if (shake && !s.shake_high && cooldown) {\n        s.shake_high = true; s.last_gesture = now;\n        if (s.last_shake != 0 && now - s.last_shake <= APP_SHAKE_PAIR_WINDOW_MS) set_event(r, \"DOUBLE_SHAKE\", \"EMERGENCY\", \"EMERGENCY_GESTURE\");\n        else set_event(r, \"SHAKE\", \"REPEAT\", \"NONE\");\n        s.last_shake = now;\n    } else if (!shake) s.shake_high = false;\n\n    if (r->response_event) return;\n    bool tilt = x->gyro_mag_dps > APP_GESTURE_GYRO_DPS && (fabsf(x->roll_deg) > APP_GESTURE_TILT_DEG || fabsf(x->pitch_deg) > APP_GESTURE_TILT_DEG);\n    if (tilt && cooldown && !s.gesture_active) { s.gesture_active = true; s.gesture_started = now; }\n    if (!tilt) {\n        if (s.gesture_active) {\n            uint32_t duration = now - s.gesture_started;\n            s.gesture_active = false;\n            if (duration >= APP_GESTURE_MIN_DURATION_MS && duration <= APP_GESTURE_MAX_DURATION_MS) {\n                s.last_gesture = now;\n                if (fabsf(x->roll_deg) > fabsf(x->pitch_deg)) {\n                    if (x->roll_deg < 0) set_event(r, \"LEFT\", \"I AM OK\", s.confirmed ? \"USER_OK\" : \"NONE\");\n                    else set_event(r, \"RIGHT\", \"I NEED HELP\", s.confirmed ? \"USER_NEEDS_HELP\" : \"NONE\");\n                } else if (x->pitch_deg < 0) set_event(r, \"FORWARD\", \"SELECT/CONFIRM\", \"NONE\");\n                else set_event(r, \"BACKWARD\", \"CANCEL/BACK\", \"NONE\");\n                if (s.confirmed) { if (x->roll_deg < 0) { s.confirmed = false; s.response_reported = false; r->system_state = SYS_USER_OK; } else { r->system_state = SYS_NEEDS_HELP; } }\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n"

+  char url[512];
+  snprintf(url, sizeof(url), "%s/external/api/logEvent?token=%s&code=%s&description=%s", APP_BLYNK_HOST, APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, event_code, message ? message : event_code);
+  request(url);
+  last_event_ms = xTaskGetTickCount() * portTICK_PERIOD_MS;
+ }
+ void blynk_publish(const imu_sample_t *s, const recognition_result_t *r)
+ {
+  if (!connected || !s || !r || strcmp(APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, "YOUR_BLYNK_DEVICE_TOKEN") == 0) return;
+  char url[768];
+  snprintf(url, sizeof(url), "%s/external/api/batch/update?token=%s&v0=%s&v1=%s&v2=%s&v3=%.3f&v4=%.3f&v5=%.3f&v6=%.2f&v7=%.2f&v8=%.2f&v9=%.1f&v10=%s&v11=%s&v12=CONNECTED&v13=%s", APP_BLYNK_HOST, APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, activity_name(r->activity), r->gesture, r->fall_confirmed ? "FALL_CONFIRMED" : "NORMAL", s->ax_g, s->ay_g, s->az_g, s->gx_dps, s->gy_dps, s->gz_dps, s->roll_deg, system_state_name(r->system_state), r->fall_confirmed ? "WAITING" : "NONE", r->event_code);
+  request(url);
+  if (r->event_code && strcmp(r->event_code, "NONE") != 0) blynk_event(r->event_code, r->action);
