  "path": "C:\\Espressif\\projects\\my_firmware\\assistive_wearable\\firmware\\README.md",
  "content": "# Smart Assistive Wearable for Visually Impaired People\n\n## 1. Project overview\n\nThis engineering prototype uses exactly two physical hardware components: an ESP32-C3 and one MPU6050. The MPU6050 is the only sensor. The phone and Blynk IoT provide communication, notifications, accessible feedback, and dashboard display.\n\n## 2. Problem and solution\n\nA visually impaired user may need simple, eyes-free control and remote assistance after a possible fall. The ESP32 samples motion locally, recognizes deliberate directional gestures and double shakes, estimates basic activity, detects multi-condition fall candidates, and sends concise Blynk events. After a confirmed fall the phone notification asks whether the user is OK. LEFT means I AM OK, RIGHT means I NEED HELP, and no response after 15 seconds means emergency.\n\nThis is not a medical, certified, or safety-critical device. MPU6050-only recognition can have false positives and false negatives.\n\n## 3. Architecture\n\n```text\nMPU6050 --I2C--> ESP32-C3 --Wi-Fi/HTTPS--> Blynk Cloud --> Blynk phone app/accessibility audio\n                 |                         |\n                 +-- filtering/gestures  +-- events and virtual datastreams\n                 +-- activity/fall state machine\n```\n\n## 4. Wiring\n\n| MPU6050 | ESP32-C3-DevKitM-1 |\n|---|---|\n| VCC | 3.3 V |\n| GND | GND |\n| SDA | GPIO4 |\n| SCL | GPIO5 |\n\nGPIO4 and GPIO5 are software-configurable in `firmware/configs/app_config.h`. The standard 7-bit address is 0x68 when AD0 is low and 0x69 when AD0 is high. Change `APP_MPU6050_ADDRESS` for the alternate address. Use the module's normal I2C pull-ups; do not connect the sensor to 5 V unless the particular breakout explicitly supports it.\n\n## 5. Configuration\n\nSet these local values in `app_config.h`:\n\n```c\n#define APP_WIFI_SSID \"Hotspot\"\n#define APP_WIFI_PASSWORD \"security\"\n#define APP_BLYNK_TEMPLATE_ID \"TMPL6H7bh4Ptg\"\n#define APP_BLYNK_TEMPLATE_NAME \"Blind Assist Wearable\"\n#define APP_BLYNK_TOKEN \"YOUR_BLYNK_DEVICE_TOKEN\"\n```\n\nThe Blynk AuthToken is device-specific and was not supplied. Replace the placeholder locally. Keep credentials out of public source control.\n\n## 6. Blynk Console setup\n\nCreate a Blynk template named `Blind Assist Wearable` with the supplied template ID. Create a device from that template and copy its AuthToken. Add these virtual datastreams:\n\n| Pin | Value | Recommended type/widget |\n|---|---|---|\n| V0 | activity | String/Label |\n| V1 | last gesture | String/Label |\n| V2 | fall status | String/LED |\n| V3–V5 | accelerometer XYZ | Double/Gauge or chart |\n| V6–V8 | gyroscope XYZ | Double/Chart |\n| V9 | roll/tilt angle | Double/Gauge |\n| V10 | system state | String/Label |\n| V11 | fall response | String/Label |\n| V12 | device status | String/LED |\n| V13 | last event | String/Label/Eventor history |\n\nUse a simple mobile layout: connection LED, activity label, gesture label, fall status LED, system state, fall response, three accelerometer values, three gyroscope values, tilt gauge, and last-event history.\n\n## 7. Events and notifications\n\nIn Blynk Console, open the template's Events page and create event codes exactly as follows: `FALL_DETECTED`, `USER_OK`, `USER_NEEDS_HELP`, `EMERGENCY_GESTURE`, `NO_RESPONSE`, `DEVICE_ONLINE`, and `DEVICE_OFFLINE`. Enable notifications for the first five. Suggested text:\n\n- FALL_DETECTED: `Possible fall detected. Are you OK?`\n- USER_OK: `User responded OK. Emergency alert cancelled.`\n- USER_NEEDS_HELP: `User needs help. Please check the device.`\n- EMERGENCY_GESTURE: `Emergency gesture detected.`\n- NO_RESPONSE: `Fall detected - no response. Emergency alert activated.`\n\nThe firmware uses Blynk's external API log-event and batch-update endpoints. Event descriptions should remain short and URL-safe; if the Console rejects a description containing punctuation or spaces, use concise event text in the Console and retain the readable phone notification there.\n\n## 8. Gesture definitions and algorithm\n\n- LEFT: roll below the calibrated neutral direction, deliberate angular motion, 30-degree tilt, 120–900 ms gesture, then return toward neutral. Outside a fall response it is a normal LEFT gesture; during a response it publishes `USER_OK`.\n- RIGHT: roll above neutral with the same timing; during a response it publishes `USER_NEEDS_HELP`.\n- DOUBLE SHAKE: two separated high-motion phases inside 900 ms; publishes `EMERGENCY_GESTURE`.\n- FORWARD/BACKWARD: pitch direction; actions SELECT/CONFIRM and CANCEL/BACK.\n\nThe recognizer uses a 100 Hz sample rate, gyro plus filtered gravity tilt, minimum/maximum duration, cooldown of 1200 ms, neutral return, and edge-triggered shake detection. Thresholds are all in `app_config.h`. The current implementation reports a gesture on the return-to-neutral edge, which prevents repeated reports while held tilted.\n\n## 9. Fall algorithm and state machine\n\n```text\nNORMAL → IMPACT_SUSPECTED → POST_IMPACT_CHECK → FALL_CONFIRMED_WAITING_RESPONSE\n                                                ├─ LEFT  → USER_OK\n                                                ├─ RIGHT → USER_NEEDS_HELP\n                                                └─ 15 s  → NO_RESPONSE emergency\n```\n\nA candidate requires dynamic acceleration over `APP_FALL_IMPACT_G`, jerk over `APP_FALL_JERK_G`, and gyro magnitude over `APP_FALL_GYRO_DPS`. Confirmation additionally requires a roll/pitch change over `APP_FALL_POSTURE_DEG`, post-impact inactivity over `APP_FALL_INACTIVITY_MS`, and the configured confirmation duration. Acceleration alone cannot confirm a fall. Ordinary walking, sitting, running, and intentional gesture motion should be collected and measured before thresholds are deployed for real use. The code suppresses fall confirmation during an intentional gesture only when the motion never satisfies the stronger impact/gyro/jerk candidate conditions; tune this with labeled trials.\n\nThe response window starts when the fall becomes confirmed. The code emits `FALL_DETECTED` once, accepts LEFT/RIGHT response gestures, and emits `NO_RESPONSE` after `APP_FALL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS`. Current firmware recognizes the response gestures through the same motion stream; because confirmation and gesture timing overlap, test and tune the mounting orientation carefully.\n\n## 10. Activity recognition\n\nThe initial classifier is rule based and uses exponentially smoothed motion energy: low energy is IDLE, moderate energy is STANDING or WALKING, high energy is RUNNING, and a sustained pitch posture is SITTING. Sitting versus standing is heuristic and highly dependent on whether the device is mounted consistently on the wrist, upper arm, chest, or torso. A later TinyML classifier is appropriate only after collecting labeled MPU6050-only windows and measuring that rules cannot meet accuracy requirements.\n\n## 11. Calibration and filtering\n\nAt startup keep the wearable still for five seconds. The driver averages gyro samples for bias and estimates the gravity vector. It uses MPU6050's digital low-pass filter and an additional gravity low-pass filter. Dynamic acceleration is the difference between acceleration magnitude and the calibrated gravity magnitude. Recalibrate after changing mounting orientation or if gyro drift is evident. A six-position accelerometer calibration can improve accuracy but is not required for the first prototype.\n\n## 12. Testing procedure\n\nDo not intentionally drop a person. Use controlled, safe test fixtures and supervised simulated motion. Record timestamps, raw/derived features, recognized state, and Blynk delivery.\n\n| Test | Expected result |\n|---|---|\n| Normal walking | No fall |\n| Left gesture | LEFT; USER_OK during fall response |\n| Right gesture | RIGHT; USER_NEEDS_HELP during response |\n| Double shake | EMERGENCY_GESTURE |\n| Forward gesture | SELECT/CONFIRM |\n| Backward gesture | CANCEL/BACK |\n| Safe simulated fall motion | Fall candidate then FALL_DETECTED if all conditions persist |\n| Fall + left | USER_OK and alert cancelled |\n| Fall + right | USER_NEEDS_HELP and emergency remains active |\n| Fall + no response | NO_RESPONSE after 15 seconds |\n| Sitting/standing | No fall; activity heuristic |\n| Running/jumping | No fall after measured threshold tuning |\n| Wi-Fi loss | Local recognition continues |\n| MPU6050 unplugged | Read errors logged; investigate before use |\n\nMeasure false positives per hour during walking, sitting, running, placing the wearable on a table, and deliberate gestures. Test Blynk notification delivery, phone sound, TalkBack/VoiceOver, and network reconnection separately.\n\n## 13. Troubleshooting and improvements\n\nIf WHO_AM_I fails, check 3.3 V, GND, SDA/SCL, pull-ups, and address 0x68/0x69. If Wi-Fi fails, verify the hotspot supports 2.4 GHz and the credentials. Replace the token placeholder before expecting cloud updates. If TLS time validation is added for production, synchronize SNTP before HTTPS and use CA/hostname validation; never disable certificate verification.\n\nFuture improvements include persisted calibration, explicit six-position calibration, a bounded network task and offline event queue, URL encoding, more robust multi-sample activity windows, Blynk online/offline events, acknowledgement controls, TinyML comparison using MPU6050-only data, and user-specific threshold profiles. Do not add another physical sensor.\n"

+ ## 7. Events and notifications
📄 edit: \\?\C:\Espressif\projects\my_firmware\assistive_wearable\firmware\README.md (L34–35)
- Rule-based detection is the initial choice because it is explainable and resource efficient. A later TinyML model should use labeled MPU6050-only windows and replace individual classifiers only if measured accuracy justifies the additional complexity.
+ In Blynk Console, open the template's Events page and create event codes exactly as follows: `FALL_DETECTED`, `USER_OK`, `USER_NEEDS_HELP`, `EMERGENCY_GESTURE`, `NO_RESPONSE`, `DEVICE_ONLINE`, and `DEVICE_OFFLINE`. Enable notifications for the first five. Suggested text:
+ - FALL_DETECTED: `Possible fall detected. Are you OK?`
+ - USER_OK: `User responded OK. Emergency alert cancelled.`
+ - USER_NEEDS_HELP: `User needs help. Please check the device.`
+ - EMERGENCY_GESTURE: `Emergency gesture detected.`
+ - NO_RESPONSE: `Fall detected - no response. Emergency alert activated.`
+ The firmware uses Blynk's external API log-event and batch-update endpoints. Event descriptions should remain short and URL-safe; if the Console rejects a description containing punctuation or spaces, use concise event text in the Console and retain the readable phone notification there.
+ ## 8. Gesture definitions and algorithm
+ - LEFT: roll below the calibrated neutral direction, deliberate angular motion, 30-degree tilt, 120–900 ms gesture, then return toward neutral. Outside a fall response it is a normal LEFT gesture; during a response it publishes `USER_OK`.
+ - RIGHT: roll above neutral with the same timing; during a response it publishes `USER_NEEDS_HELP`.
+ - DOUBLE SHAKE: two separated high-motion phases inside 900 ms; publishes `EMERGENCY_GESTURE`.
+ - FORWARD/BACKWARD: pitch direction; actions SELECT/CONFIRM and CANCEL/BACK.
+ The recognizer uses a 100 Hz sample rate, gyro plus filtered gravity tilt, minimum/maximum duration, cooldown of 1200 ms, neutral return, and edge-triggered shake detection. Thresholds are all in `app_config.h`. The current implementation reports a gesture on the return-to-neutral edge, which prevents repeated reports while held tilted.
+ ## 9. Fall algorithm and state machine
+ ```text
+ NORMAL → IMPACT_SUSPECTED → POST_IMPACT_CHECK → FALL_CONFIRMED_WAITING_RESPONSE
+  ├─ LEFT → USER_OK
+  ├─ RIGHT → USER_NEEDS_HELP
+  └─ 15 s → NO_RESPONSE emergency
+ ```
+ A candidate requires dynamic acceleration over `APP_FALL_IMPACT_G`, jerk over `APP_FALL_JERK_G`, and gyro magnitude over `APP_FALL_GYRO_DPS`. Confirmation additionally requires a roll/pitch change over `APP_FALL_POSTURE_DEG`, post-impact inactivity over `APP_FALL_INACTIVITY_MS`, and the configured confirmation duration. Acceleration alone cannot confirm a fall. Ordinary walking, sitting, running, and intentional gesture motion should be collected and measured before thresholds are deployed for real use. The code suppresses fall confirmation during an intentional gesture only when the motion never satisfies the stronger impact/gyro/jerk candidate conditions; tune this with labeled trials.
+ The response window starts when the fall becomes confirmed. The code emits `FALL_DETECTED` once, accepts LEFT/RIGHT response gestures, and emits `NO_RESPONSE` after `APP_FALL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS`. Current firmware recognizes the response gestures through the same motion stream; because confirmation and gesture timing overlap, test and tune the mounting orientation carefully.
+ ## 10. Activity recognition
+ The initial classifier is rule based and uses exponentially smoothed motion energy: low energy is IDLE, moderate energy is STANDING or WALKING, high energy is RUNNING, and a sustained pitch posture is SITTING. Sitting versus standing is heuristic and highly dependent on whether the device is mounted consistently on the wrist, upper arm, chest, or torso. A later TinyML classifier is appropriate only after collecting labeled MPU6050-only windows and measuring that rules cannot meet accuracy requirements.
+ ## 11. Calibration and filtering
+ At startup keep the wearable still for five seconds. The driver averages gyro samples for bias and estimates the gravity vector. It uses MPU6050's digital low-pass filter and an additional gravity low-pass filter. Dynamic acceleration is the difference between acceleration magnitude and the calibrated gravity magnitude. Recalibrate after changing mounting orientation or if gyro drift is evident. A six-position accelerometer calibration can improve accuracy but is not required for the first prototype.
+ ## 12. Testing procedure
+ Do not intentionally drop a person. Use controlled, safe test fixtures and supervised simulated motion. Record timestamps, raw/derived features, recognized state, and Blynk delivery.
+ | Test | Expected result |
+ |---|---|
+ | Normal walking | No fall |
+ | Left gesture | LEFT; USER_OK during fall response |
+ | Right gesture | RIGHT; USER_NEEDS_HELP during response |
+ | Double shake | EMERGENCY_GESTURE |
+ | Forward gesture | SELECT/CONFIRM |
+ | Backward gesture | CANCEL/BACK |
+ | Safe simulated fall motion | Fall candidate then FALL_DETECTED if all conditions persist |
+ | Fall + left | USER_OK and alert cancelled |
+ | Fall + right | USER_NEEDS_HELP and emergency remains active |
+ | Fall + no response | NO_RESPONSE after 15 seconds |
+ | Sitting/standing | No fall; activity heuristic |
+ | Running/jumping | No fall after measured threshold tuning |
+ | Wi-Fi loss | Local recognition continues |
+ | MPU6050 unplugged | Read errors logged; investigate before use |
+ Measure false positives per hour during walking, sitting, running, placing the wearable on a table, and deliberate gestures. Test Blynk notification delivery, phone sound, TalkBack/VoiceOver, and network reconnection separately.
+ ## 13. Troubleshooting and improvements
+ If WHO_AM_I fails, check 3.3 V, GND, SDA/SCL, pull-ups, and address 0x68/0x69. If Wi-Fi fails, verify the hotspot supports 2.4 GHz and the credentials. Replace the token placeholder before expecting cloud updates. If TLS time validation is added for production, synchronize SNTP before HTTPS and use CA/hostname validation; never disable certificate verification.
+ Future improvements include persisted calibration, explicit six-position calibration, a bounded network task and offline event queue, URL encoding, more robust multi-sample activity windows, Blynk online/offline events, acknowledgement controls, TinyML comparison using MPU6050-only data, and user-specific threshold profiles. Do not add another physical sensor.

The initialization boot loop was fixed.

Changes made

  • Added an effective single-initialization guard for the MPU6050 I²C bus.
  • Added cleanup after partial I²C initialization failures.
  • Added automatic probing of both MPU6050 addresses:

- 0x68 - 0x69

  • Removed the fatal reboot behavior when the sensor is not detected.
  • Added retry behavior every five seconds.
  • Added clearer diagnostics for:

- 3.3 V - GND - SDA GPIO4 - SCL GPIO5 - MPU6050 address configuration

Verification

The firmware now:

  • Builds successfully.
  • Flashes successfully.
  • No longer enters a reboot loop.
  • Remains running while retrying MPU6050 initialization.

However, the monitor still shows that the MPU6050 is not responding:

No MPU6050 response at 0x68; probing 0x69
MPU6050 WHO_AM_I failed: 0x00

This indicates a physical I²C communication problem rather than a duplicate-initialization problem.

Please verify:

| MPU6050 | ESP32-C3 | |---|---| | VCC | 3.3 V | | GND | GND | | SDA | GPIO4 | | SCL | GPIO5 |

Also check:

  1. The MPU6050 breakout is powered from 3.3 V.
  2. SDA and SCL are not swapped.
  3. The module has I²C pull-ups.
  4. AD0 determines the address:

- AD0 low → 0x68 - AD0 high → 0x69

  1. The module is not a different sensor board with an incompatible address or damaged I²C interface.
  2. The ESP32 flash-size warning is unrelated to the MPU6050 issue, but the project should later be configured for the board’s actual 4 MB flash size.

The firmware should not be considered runtime-successful yet because no valid WHOAMI response was received.

solve the error

// ===== C:/Espressif/esp-idf-release-v5.5/components/esp_driver_i2c/include/driver/i2c_master.h =====
  223   *      - ESP_OK: I2C master receive success
  224   *      - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: I2C master receive parameter invalid.
  225   *      - ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT: Operation timeout(larger than xfer_timeout_ms) because the bus is busy or hardware crash.
  226   */
  227  esp_err_t i2c_master_receive(i2c_master_dev_handle_t i2c_dev, uint8_t *read_buffer, size_t read_size, int xfer_timeout_ms);
  228  
  229  /**
  230   * @brief Probe I2C address, if address is correct and ACK is received, this function will return ESP_OK.
  231   *
  232   * @param[in] bus_handle I2C master device handle that created by `i2c_master_bus_add_device`.
  233   * @param[in] address I2C device address that you want to probe.
  234   * @param[in] xfer_timeout_ms Wait timeout, in ms. Note: -1 means wait forever (Not recommended in this function).
  235   *
  236   * @attention Pull-ups must be connected to the SCL and SDA pins when this function is called. If you get `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT
  237   * while `xfer_timeout_ms` was parsed correctly, you should check the pull-up resistors. If you do not have proper resistors nearby.
  238   * `flags.enable_internal_pullup` is also acceptable.
  239   *
  240   * @note The principle of this function is to sent device address with a write command. If the device on your I2C bus, there would be an ACK signal and function
  241   * returns `ESP_OK`. If the device is not on your I2C bus, there would be a NACK signal and function returns `ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND`. `ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT` is not an expected
  242   * failure, which indicated that the i2c probe not works properly, usually caused by pull-up resistors not be connected properly. Suggestion check data on SDA/SCL line
  243   * to see whether there is ACK/NACK signal is on line when i2c probe function fails.
  244   *
  245   * @note There are lots of I2C devices all over the world, we assume that not all I2C device support the behavior like `device_address+nack/ack`.
  246   * So, if the on line data is strange and no ack/nack got respond. Please check the device datasheet.
  247   *
  248   * @return
  249   *      - ESP_OK: I2C device probe successfully
  250   *      - ESP_ERR_NOT_FOUND: I2C probe failed, doesn't find the device with specific address you gave.
  251   *      - ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT: Operation timeout(larger than xfer_timeout_ms) because the bus is busy or hardware crash.
  252   */
  253  esp_err_t i2c_master_probe(i2c_master_bus_handle_t bus_handle, uint16_t address, int xfer_timeout_ms);
  254  
  255  /**
  256   * @brief Execute a series of pre-defined I2C operations.
  257   *
  258   * This function processes a list of I2C operations, such as start, write, read, and stop,
  259   * according to the user-defined `i2c_operation_job_t` array. It performs these operations
  260   * sequentially on the specified I2C master device.
  261   *
  262   * @param[in] i2c_dev           Handle to the I2C master device.
  263   * @param[in] i2c_operation     Pointer to an array of user-defined I2C operation jobs.
  264   *                              Each job specifies a command and associated parameters.
  265   * @param[in] operation_list_num The number of operations in the `i2c_operation` array.
  266   * @param[in] xfer_timeout_ms   Timeout for the transaction, in milliseconds.
  267   *
  268   * @return
  269   *  - ESP_OK: Transaction completed successfully.
  270   *  - ESP_ERR_INVALID_ARG: One or more arguments are invalid.
  271   *  - ESP_ERR_TIMEOUT: Transaction timed out.
  272   *  - ESP_FAIL: Other error during transaction.
  273   *
  274   * @note The `ack_value` field in the READ operation must be set to `I2C_NACK_VAL` if the next
  275   *       operation is a STOP command.
  276   */
  277  esp_err_t i2c_master_execute_defined_operations(i2c_master_dev_handle_t i2c_dev, i2c_operation_job_t *i2c_operation, size_t operation_list_num, int xfer_timeout_ms);

- 0x68 - 0x69

AI

The firmware is not the current blocker. The ESP32 serial connection is.

Current evidence:

  • Build succeeds.
  • GPIO mapping is correct:

- SCL → GPIO4 - SDA → GPIO5

  • COM3 is busy or inaccessible.
  • COM4 and COM5 were detected as ports but did not respond as ESP32 devices.
  • No port could provide chip information.

Use this recovery procedure:

  1. Close all serial terminals and monitors.
  2. Disconnect the ESP32.
  3. In Windows Device Manager, note the current COM ports.
  4. Reconnect the ESP32 with a known data-capable USB cable.
  5. Identify which COM port appears newly.
  6. Hold the ESP32-C3 BOOT button while reconnecting or pressing RESET.
  7. Release BOOT after the board enters download mode.
  8. Make sure no application owns the detected port.
  9. Flash using that port instead of COM3.

Do not change the MPU6050 firmware further until the ESP32 can be identified and flashed. After the board is accessible, the expected runtime output should show:

I2C probe 0x68: ...
I2C probe 0x69: ...
WHO_AM_I read ...
MPU6050 ready ...

If neither COM4 nor COM5 responds, the likely causes are:

  • USB cable is charge-only.
  • Missing or incorrect USB/UART driver.
  • Board is not powered or is in the wrong boot mode.
  • Another application has claimed the port.
  • The ports belong to unrelated devices.

Once the correct ESP32 port is available, use:

  • [Retry]
  • [Flash to device]

📝 Code changes (1 chunk)
📄 edit: \\?\C:\Espressif\projects\my_firmware\assistive_wearable\docs\overview.md (L1–1)
+ # Smart Assistive Wearable
+ This ESP32-C3 firmware uses one MPU6050 as its only physical sensor. It samples motion over I2C, calibrates the stationary device, estimates activity and orientation, recognizes intentional gestures, and evaluates multi-condition fall candidates. Blynk IoT provides Wi-Fi telemetry, event notifications, dashboard status, and phone accessibility feedback.
+ ## Hardware
+ - MPU6050 VCC → ESP32-C3 3.3 V
+ - MPU6050 GND → ESP32-C3 GND
+ - MPU6050 SDA → ESP32-C3 GPIO5
+ - MPU6050 SCL → ESP32-C3 GPIO4
+ - Default MPU6050 address: 0x68; AD0 high selects 0x69
+ The smartphone and Blynk Cloud are communication and user-interface layers only. This prototype is not medically certified or safety-critical.
+ ## Firmware modules
+ `firmware/platforms/esp32/mpu6050.c` implements ESP-IDF I2C access, address probing, calibration, filtering, and feature extraction. `firmware/services/recognizer.c` implements gesture, activity, fall, and response state machines. `firmware/platforms/esp32/blynk_client.c` implements Wi-Fi and Blynk HTTP telemetry/events. `firmware/app/app.c` orchestrates startup and the fixed-rate processing loop.
+ Build with ESP-IDF 5.5 for ESP32-C3. Configure local Wi-Fi and Blynk credentials in `firmware/configs/app_config.h`, keep the device still during the five-second startup calibration, and validate all thresholds with supervised, controlled tests before considering any field use.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Audit the current recognizer, application loop, and Blynk client for precision and state-retention defects. Preserve the confirmed ESP32-C3 hardware configuration: MPU6050 only, SCL on GPIO4, SDA on GPIO5, 100 Hz sampling, and automatic address detection at 0x68/0x69.
2. Correct the recognizer state machine so fall-response gestures are processed while a fall is waiting for a response. Persist the current system state, last gesture, last event, fall score, response deadline, and emergency state instead of resetting them on every sample.
3. Improve gesture and fall precision using calibrated neutral orientation, filtered multi-sample windows, hysteresis, minimum/maximum durations, neutral return, cooldowns, and explicit separation between shake, tilt, and fall motion. Keep default confirmation at 3 seconds and the response window at 15 seconds, with all thresholds configurable and documented.
4. Make Blynk payloads stable, readable, and event-driven. Publish persistent values to V0–V13, send each emergency event once, URL-encode text and credentials, apply HTTPS certificate validation, rate-limit telemetry to approximately 1 Hz, and retain the latest critical event for reconnection delivery.
5. Define exact Blynk text and event messages: `Possible fall detected. Are you OK?`, `User responded OK. Emergency alert cancelled.`, `User needs help. Please check the user.`, `Emergency gesture detected.`, and `Fall detected. No response received. Emergency activated.` Document that Blynk supplies notification text while TalkBack, VoiceOver, and notification read-aloud are handled by the smartphone.
6. Remove real credentials from committed source where practical. Keep the supplied template ID and template name, use `Hotspot` and `security` only as local configuration defaults, keep the Blynk device token as a required local placeholder until supplied, and add ignore/example guidance for local secrets.
7. Expand the project documentation with an implementation summary, file/module map, wiring, calibration, algorithms, state transitions, Blynk Console setup, exact datastream/event mappings, accessibility configuration, test procedure, known limitations, and the explicit non-medical-prototype warning.
8. Build, flash, and monitor the ESP32-C3 on the confirmed device port. Verify valid MPU6050 WHO_AM_I, stable calibration, no reboot loop, correct gesture/fall state transitions, correct Blynk text, and no repeated event notifications. Tune thresholds only after controlled labeled tests at the final wearable mounting position.

**Alternatives:** If gesture precision remains poor, first collect timestamped MPU6050-only samples at the actual mounting location and tune the rule-based windows before considering a small TinyML classifier. If Blynk external API requests reject encoded text or event codes, verify the Console event-code spelling and current API endpoint format while retaining the same internal event interface. If HTTPS certificate validation fails because system time is unset, synchronize SNTP before cloud requests and never disable certificate verification. If the MPU6050 becomes unavailable, keep the firmware in a recoverable degraded state with local diagnostics and retry using the existing 0x68/0x69 probing; do not add another physical sensor. If smartphone notifications are not spoken automatically, configure Android TalkBack, iOS VoiceOver, or the operating system's notification read-aloud/accessibility service; Blynk itself should remain responsible only for delivering concise notification text.

  "content": "#include \"recognizer.h\"\n#include \"app_config.h\"\n#include <math.h>\n#include <string.h>\n\ntypedef struct {\n    float last_accel;\n    float max_dynamic;\n    uint32_t last_shake;\n    uint32_t last_gesture;\n    uint32_t candidate_at;\n    uint32_t active_motion;\n    uint32_t gesture_started;\n    float energy;\n    bool candidate;\n    bool confirmed;\n    bool response_reported;\n    bool gesture_active;\n    bool shake_high;\n} state_t;\nstatic state_t s;\n\nvoid recognizer_init(void) { memset(&s, 0, sizeof(s)); }\n\nconst char *activity_name(activity_t a)\n{\n    static const char *names[] = {\"Idle\", \"Standing\", \"Walking\", \"Running\", \"Sitting\", \"Fall candidate\", \"Fall confirmed\"};\n    return a <= ACT_FALL_CONFIRMED ? names[a] : \"Unknown\";\n}\n\nconst char *system_state_name(system_state_t state)\n{\n    static const char *names[] = {\"MONITORING\", \"WAITING_FOR_RESPONSE\", \"USER_OK\", \"NEEDS_HELP\", \"EMERGENCY\", \"NO_RESPONSE\"};\n    return state <= SYS_NO_RESPONSE ? names[state] : \"UNKNOWN\";\n}\n\nstatic void set_event(recognition_result_t *r, const char *gesture, const char *action, const char *event)\n{\n    r->gesture = gesture;\n    r->action = action;\n    r->event_code = event;\n    r->response_event = true;\n}\n\nvoid recognizer_update(const imu_sample_t *x, uint32_t now, recognition_result_t *r)\n{\n    memset(r, 0, sizeof(*r));\n    r->gesture = \"NONE\";\n    r->action = \"NONE\";\n    r->event_code = \"NONE\";\n    r->activity = ACT_IDLE;\n    r->system_state = SYS_MONITORING;\n    r->action_code = ACTION_NONE;\n\n    float jerk = fabsf(x->accel_mag_g - s.last_accel);\n    s.last_accel = x->accel_mag_g;\n    s.energy = 0.95f * s.energy + 0.05f * (x->dynamic_accel_g + x->gyro_mag_dps / 180.0f);\n\n    if (!s.candidate && !s.confirmed &&\n        x->dynamic_accel_g > APP_FALL_IMPACT_G &&\n        x->gyro_mag_dps > APP_FALL_GYRO_DPS && jerk > APP_FALL_JERK_G) {\n        s.candidate = true;\n        s.candidate_at = now;\n        s.active_motion = now;\n        s.max_dynamic = x->dynamic_accel_g;\n    }\n\n    if (s.candidate || s.confirmed) {\n        if (x->gyro_mag_dps > 35.0f || x->dynamic_accel_g > 0.25f) s.active_motion = now;\n        if (x->dynamic_accel_g > s.max_dynamic) s.max_dynamic = x->dynamic_accel_g;\n        bool posture_change = fabsf(x->roll_deg) > APP_FALL_POSTURE_DEG || fabsf(x->pitch_deg) > APP_FALL_POSTURE_DEG;\n        bool inactive = now - s.active_motion > APP_FALL_INACTIVITY_MS;\n        if (s.candidate && posture_change && inactive && now - s.candidate_at >= APP_FALL_CONFIRM_MS) s.confirmed = true;\n        if (s.candidate && !s.confirmed && now - s.candidate_at > APP_FALL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS) s.candidate = false;\n    }\n\n    if (s.confirmed && !s.response_reported) {\n        r->activity = ACT_FALL_CONFIRMED;\n        r->system_state = SYS_FALL_WAITING;\n        r->fall_confirmed = true;\n        r->fall_score = 1.0f;\n        set_event(r, \"FALL_CONFIRMED\", \"ARE YOU OK?\", \"FALL_DETECTED\");\n        r->action_code = ACTION_FALL_DETECTED;\n        s.response_reported = true;\n        return;\n    }\n\n    if (s.confirmed) {\n        r->activity = ACT_FALL_CONFIRMED;\n        r->system_state = SYS_FALL_WAITING;\n        r->fall_confirmed = true;\n        r->fall_score = 1.0f;\n        if (now - s.candidate_at >= APP_FALL_CONFIRM_MS + APP_FALL_RESPONSE_TIMEOUT_MS) {\n            r->system_state = SYS_NO_RESPONSE;\n            set_event(r, \"NO_RESPONSE\", \"EMERGENCY\", \"NO_RESPONSE\");\n            r->action_code = ACTION_NO_RESPONSE;\n            s.confirmed = false;\n            s.candidate = false;\n            s.response_reported = false;\n        }\n        return;\n    }\n\n    if (s.candidate) {\n        r->activity = ACT_FALL_CANDIDATE;\n        r->system_state = SYS_FALL_WAITING;\n        r->fall_score = 0.6f;\n        return;\n    }\n\n    if (s.energy < 0.035f) r->activity = ACT_IDLE;\n    else if (s.energy > 0.65f) r->activity = ACT_RUNNING;\n    else if (s.energy > 0.16f) r->activity = ACT_WALKING;\n    else if (fabsf(x->pitch_deg) > 45.0f) r->activity = ACT_SITTING;\n    else r->activity = ACT_STANDING;\n\n    bool cooldown = now - s.last_gesture >= APP_GESTURE_COOLDOWN_MS;\n    bool shake = x->gyro_mag_dps > APP_SHAKE_GYRO_DPS && x->dynamic_accel_g > APP_SHAKE_DYNAMIC_G;\n    if (shake && !s.shake_high && cooldown) {\n        s.shake_high = true;\n        s.last_gesture = now;\n        if (s.last_shake != 0 && now - s.last_shake <= APP_SHAKE_PAIR_WINDOW_MS) {\n            set_event(r, \"DOUBLE_SHAKE\", \"EMERGENCY\", \"EMERGENCY_GESTURE\");\n            r->action_code = ACTION_DOUBLE_SHAKE_EMERGENCY;\n        } else {\n            set_event(r, \"SHAKE\", \"REPEAT\", \"NONE\");\n            r->action_code = ACTION_SINGLE_SHAKE_REPEAT;\n        }\n        s.last_shake = now;\n    } else if (!shake) {\n        s.shake_high = false;\n    }\n\n    if (r->response_event) return;\n\n    bool tilt = x->gyro_mag_dps > APP_GESTURE_GYRO_DPS &&\n                (fabsf(x->roll_deg) > APP_GESTURE_TILT_DEG || fabsf(x->pitch_deg) > APP_GESTURE_TILT_DEG);\n    if (tilt && cooldown && !s.gesture_active) {\n        s.gesture_active = true;\n        s.gesture_started = now;\n    }\n    if (!tilt && s.gesture_active) {\n        uint32_t duration = now - s.gesture_started;\n        s.gesture_active = false;\n        if (duration >= APP_GESTURE_MIN_DURATION_MS && duration <= APP_GESTURE_MAX_DURATION_MS) {\n            s.last_gesture = now;\n            if (fabsf(x->roll_deg) > fabsf(x->pitch_deg)) {\n                if (x->roll_deg < 0) {\n                    set_event(r, \"LEFT\", \"I AM OK\", s.confirmed ? \"USER_OK\" : \"NONE\");\n                    r->action_code = ACTION_LEFT_OK;\n                } else {\n                    set_event(r, \"RIGHT\", \"I NEED HELP\", s.confirmed ? \"USER_NEEDS_HELP\" : \"NONE\");\n                    r->action_code = ACTION_RIGHT_HELP;\n                }\n            } else if (x->pitch_deg < 0) {\n                set_event(r, \"FORWARD\", \"SELECT/CONFIRM\", \"NONE\");\n                r->action_code = ACTION_FORWARD_CONFIRM;\n            } else {\n                set_event(r, \"BACKWARD\", \"CANCEL/BACK\", \"NONE\");\n                r->action_code = ACTION_BACKWARD_CANCEL;\n            }\n            if (s.confirmed && x->roll_deg < 0) {\n                s.confirmed = false;\n                s.response_reported = false;\n                r->system_state = SYS_USER_OK;\n            } else if (s.confirmed) {\n                r->system_state = SYS_NEEDS_HELP;\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n"
}

AI

Implemented integer action values for Blynk.

New Blynk datastream

Use:

V14 = Action Code
Type: Integer

Action codes

| Code | Action | |---:|---| | 0 | None | | 1 | Left / User OK | | 2 | Right / User needs help | | 3 | Double shake / Emergency | | 4 | Forward / Confirm | | 5 | Backward / Cancel | | 6 | Single shake / Repeat | | 7 | Fall detected | | 8 | No response |

The firmware now sends V14 with every Blynk telemetry update:

V14 = integer action code

Text values remain available on the existing streams, while V14 provides a stable value that can be used by Blynk automations, datastream logic, or external systems.

Validation

  • Fixed recognizer syntax errors caused by missing braces.
  • Corrected shake action handling.
  • Corrected directional action handling.
  • Added the integer action-code field.
  • Syntax check passed.
  • Full ESP32-C3 build passed.

The updated firmware is built but has not yet been flashed after this change.

Integer Blynk action codes

The firmware now publishes the action value on:

V14

Configure V14 in Blynk as an Integer datastream.

| V14 | Meaning | |---:|---| | 0 | None | | 1 | Left / User OK | | 2 | Right / User needs help | | 3 | Double shake / Emergency | | 4 | Forward / Confirm | | 5 | Backward / Cancel | | 6 | Single shake / Repeat | | 7 | Fall detected | | 8 | No response |

The action code is sent with every Blynk telemetry update and can be used with Blynk automations or value-based widgets.

Documentation updated

The README and Doxygen overview now document V14 and all integer action mappings.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Replace Blynk Cloud with a local ESP32-hosted web interface while keeping Wi-Fi station mode on the existing `Hotspot` network. No Blynk token, cloud endpoint, Blynk event, or Blynk datastream will remain.
2. Remove the Blynk client and its HTTP-client dependency from the application and build configuration.
3. Add an ESP-IDF-native HTTP server that serves an embedded HTML page and exposes JSON state/history endpoints.
4. Add a thread-safe application snapshot containing activity, gesture, action code, fall state, response state, sensor values, Wi-Fi status, IP address, and recent events.
5. Update the application loop to publish snapshots and append only edge-triggered events, preventing duplicate history entries or repeated spoken alerts.
6. Create a responsive HTML/JavaScript dashboard showing the current state, event history, integer action codes, MPU6050 values, and Wi-Fi address. The page will poll the ESP32 locally at a default 500 ms interval.
7. Add browser-based spoken feedback using the Web Speech API. The page will speak only new high-priority events and state transitions, with an explicit `Enable spoken alerts` button because mobile browsers generally require user interaction before speech is allowed.
8. Update configuration and documentation for the local web architecture, including the page URL, Wi-Fi behavior, speech limitations, endpoint format, action-code meanings, wiring, calibration, fall/gesture behavior, testing, and the warning that this remains a non-medical prototype.
9. Correct the ESP32-C3 flash-size configuration to match the board’s confirmed 4 MB flash and remove the old 2 MB image-header warning.
10. Build, flash, and monitor the revised firmware. Verify MPU6050 detection on SCL GPIO4/SDA GPIO5, stable Wi-Fi startup, page access, correct state updates, one spoken announcement per event, and continued local operation when Wi-Fi is unavailable.

Defaults: the ESP32 remains a Wi-Fi station connected to `Hotspot`; the web page is served on port 80; no additional physical hardware is added; speech is produced by the smartphone/browser rather than the ESP32; browser polling is used instead of WebSockets for reliability; the page is local-network only and has no cloud authentication, so it should not be exposed to an untrusted network.

**Alternatives:** If the smartphone browser does not support Web Speech API or suspends speech in the background, keep the visible event history and add a clear browser compatibility note; no speaker can be added because the hardware restriction is ESP32 plus MPU6050 only. If station-mode Wi-Fi is unavailable, keep local recognition running and optionally add a deferred fallback SoftAP mode without changing the sensor design. If HTTP polling is too slow, replace only the update transport with Server-Sent Events while retaining the same JSON snapshot and event-ID protocol. If the web server cannot embed the HTML through CMake, store the page as a compiled C string or use a separate ESP-IDF embedded-file component, but do not restore Blynk or cloud dependencies.

# Plan
## Plan
1. Remove all Blynk Cloud code, credentials, template settings, event calls, and HTTP-client dependencies while preserving the ESP32-C3 + MPU6050-only design.
2. Add a local ESP-IDF Wi-Fi station and HTTP server. The ESP32 will serve the dashboard at `http://<device-ip>/` after joining the configured `Hotspot` network using DHCP.
3. Add a thread-safe local state and bounded event-history service. Preserve integer action codes, sensor values, fall state, activity, Wi-Fi status, timestamps, and monotonically increasing event IDs.
4. Embed a responsive HTML dashboard in the firmware. It will poll `/api/state` and `/api/history`, display readable status and integer actions, and show the device IP and connection state.
5. Add phone-speaker speech using the browser Web Speech API. The page will announce Wi-Fi connection and new actions once per event, with an explicit Enable Spoken Alerts button because mobile browsers require user interaction before speech and may suspend speech in the background.
6. Integrate the web state service into the existing application loop without blocking 100 Hz MPU6050 processing. Record only edge-triggered actions and state transitions so polling does not duplicate events or spoken announcements.
7. Update ESP-IDF component registration, embedded-file configuration, local web settings, and the confirmed 4 MB flash configuration. Keep SCL on GPIO4 and SDA on GPIO5, and preserve automatic MPU6050 address detection at 0x68/0x69.
8. Rewrite the project documentation to describe the local web architecture, endpoints, dashboard fields, action codes, speech behavior, Wi-Fi setup, wiring, calibration, algorithms, testing, security limitations, and non-medical prototype status.
9. Build, flash, monitor, and test the running device. Verify MPU6050 initialization, Wi-Fi IP assignment, dashboard access from a phone on the same network, one speech announcement per event, and continued local recognition when Wi-Fi is unavailable.

Defaults: station-only Wi-Fi with DHCP on `Hotspot`, HTTP port 80, 500 ms browser polling, a bounded in-memory history, no cloud service, no authentication, no additional physical hardware, and browser/phone speech rather than an ESP32 speaker. The local webpage must not be exposed to an untrusted network because it has no authentication or encryption.

**Alternatives:** If mobile browser speech is blocked or suspended in the background, retain the visible event history and document that the page must remain open/active with speech enabled; Blynk and cloud services will not be restored. If station Wi-Fi is unavailable, keep MPU6050 recognition running and optionally add a bounded SoftAP fallback later without changing the sensor design. If polling is insufficient, replace only the transport with Server-Sent Events while preserving the same snapshot schema and event IDs. If the phone cannot reach the device, verify both devices are on the same hotspot subnet and that client isolation is disabled. If the MPU6050 stops responding, retain the existing recoverable retry path and check 3.3 V, GND, GPIO4 SCL, GPIO5 SDA, pull-ups, and AD0; do not add another physical sensor.

// ===== C:/Espressif/esp-idf-release-v5.5/components/esp_http_server/include/esp_http_server.h =====
  259       * closing the socket. This is a place for custom de-init code common to all sockets.
  260       *
  261       * The server will only close the socket if no custom session closing callback is set.
  262       * If a custom callback is used, `close(sockfd)` should be called in here for most cases.
  263       *
  264       * Set the user or transport context to NULL if it was freed here, so the server does not
  265       * try to free it again.
  266       *
  267       * This function is run for all terminated sessions, including sessions where the socket
  268       * was closed by the network stack - that is, the file descriptor may not be valid anymore.
  269       */

  273       * URI matcher function.
  274       *
  275       * Called when searching for a matching URI:
  276       *     1) whose request handler is to be executed right
  277       *        after an HTTP request is successfully parsed
  278       *     2) in order to prevent duplication while registering
  279       *        a new URI handler using `httpd_register_uri_handler()`
  280       *
  281       * Available options are:
  282       *     1) NULL : Internally do basic matching using `strncmp()`
  283       *     2) `httpd_uri_match_wildcard()` : URI wildcard matcher
  284       *
  285       * Users can implement their own matching functions (See description
  286       * of the `httpd_uri_match_func_t` function prototype)
  287       */
  288      httpd_uri_match_func_t uri_match_fn;

menu "Example Configuration"

    config EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH
        bool "Basic Authentication"
        default n
        help
            Basic Authentication is a method for an HTTP user agent (e.g. a web browser)
            to provide a user name and password when making a request. It is the simplest
            technique for enforcing access controls to web resources. because it doesn't
            require cookies, session identifiers, or login pages; rather, it uses standard
            fields in the HTTP header.
            Note that, Basic Authentication is not encrypted channel and also easy to retrieve
            credentials as they are sent in plain text format.

    config EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH_USERNAME
        string "Basic Authenticate User Name"
        depends on EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH
        default "ESP32"
        help
            The client's user name which used for basic authenticate.

        string "Basic Authenticate Password"
        depends on EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH
        default "ESP32Webserver"

/* Simple HTTP Server Example

#include <string.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <unistd.h>
#include <esp_log.h>
#include <nvs_flash.h>
#include <sys/param.h>
#include "esp_netif.h"
#include "protocol_examples_common.h"
#include "protocol_examples_utils.h"
#include "esp_tls_crypto.h"

#include "esp_check.h"

#define EXAMPLE_HTTP_QUERY_KEY_MAX_LEN  (64)

/* A simple example that demonstrates how to create GET and POST
 * handlers for the web server.
 */

static const char *TAG = "example";

#if CONFIG_EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH

typedef struct {
    char    *username;
    char    *password;
} basic_auth_info_t;

#define HTTPD_401      "401 UNAUTHORIZED"           /*!< HTTP Response 401 */

static char *http_auth_basic(const char *username, const char *password)
{
    size_t out;
    char *user_info = NULL;
    char *digest = NULL;
    size_t n = 0;
    int rc = asprintf(&user_info, "%s:%s", username, password);
    if (rc < 0) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "asprintf() returned: %d", rc);
        return NULL;
    }

    if (!user_info) {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "No enough memory for user information");
        return NULL;
    }
    esp_crypto_base64_encode(NULL, 0, &n, (const unsigned char *)user_info, strlen(user_info));

    /* 6: The length of the "Basic " string
     * n: Number of bytes for a base64 encode format
     * 1: Number of bytes for a reserved which be used to fill zero
    */
    digest = calloc(1, 6 + n + 1);
    if (digest) {
        strcpy(digest, "Basic ");
        esp_crypto_base64_encode((unsigned char *)digest + 6, n, &out, (const unsigned char *)user_info, strlen(user_info));
    }
    free(user_info);
    return digest;
}

/* An HTTP GET handler */
static esp_err_t basic_auth_get_handler(httpd_req_t *req)
{
    char *buf = NULL;
    size_t buf_len = 0;
    basic_auth_info_t *basic_auth_info = req->user_ctx;

    buf_len = httpd_req_get_hdr_value_len(req, "Authorization") + 1;
    if (buf_len > 1) {
        buf = calloc(1, buf_len);
        if (!buf) {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "No enough memory for basic authorization");
            return ESP_ERR_NO_MEM;
        }

        if (httpd_req_get_hdr_value_str(req, "Authorization", buf, buf_len) == ESP_OK) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found header => Authorization: %s", buf);
        } else {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "No auth value received");
        }

        char *auth_credentials = http_auth_basic(basic_auth_info->username, basic_auth_info->password);
        if (!auth_credentials) {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "No enough memory for basic authorization credentials");
            free(buf);
            return ESP_ERR_NO_MEM;
        }

        if (strncmp(auth_credentials, buf, buf_len)) {
            ESP_LOGE(TAG, "Not authenticated");
            httpd_resp_set_status(req, HTTPD_401);
            httpd_resp_set_type(req, "application/json");
            httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "Connection", "keep-alive");
            httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "WWW-Authenticate", "Basic realm=\"Hello\"");
            httpd_resp_send(req, NULL, 0);
        } else {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Authenticated!");
            char *basic_auth_resp = NULL;
            httpd_resp_set_status(req, HTTPD_200);
            httpd_resp_set_type(req, "application/json");
            httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "Connection", "keep-alive");
            int rc = asprintf(&basic_auth_resp, "{\"authenticated\": true,\"user\": \"%s\"}", basic_auth_info->username);
            if (rc < 0) {
                ESP_LOGE(TAG, "asprintf() returned: %d", rc);
                free(auth_credentials);
                return ESP_FAIL;
            }
            if (!basic_auth_resp) {
                ESP_LOGE(TAG, "No enough memory for basic authorization response");
                free(auth_credentials);
                free(buf);
                return ESP_ERR_NO_MEM;
            }
            httpd_resp_send(req, basic_auth_resp, strlen(basic_auth_resp));
            free(basic_auth_resp);
        }
        free(auth_credentials);
        free(buf);
    } else {
        ESP_LOGE(TAG, "No auth header received");
        httpd_resp_set_status(req, HTTPD_401);
        httpd_resp_set_type(req, "application/json");
        httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "Connection", "keep-alive");
        httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "WWW-Authenticate", "Basic realm=\"Hello\"");
        httpd_resp_send(req, NULL, 0);
    }

    return ESP_OK;
}

static httpd_uri_t basic_auth = {
    .uri       = "/basic_auth",
    .method    = HTTP_GET,
    .handler   = basic_auth_get_handler,
};

static void httpd_register_basic_auth(httpd_handle_t server)
{
    basic_auth_info_t *basic_auth_info = calloc(1, sizeof(basic_auth_info_t));
    if (basic_auth_info) {
        basic_auth_info->username = CONFIG_EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH_USERNAME;
        basic_auth_info->password = CONFIG_EXAMPLE_BASIC_AUTH_PASSWORD;

        basic_auth.user_ctx = basic_auth_info;
        httpd_register_uri_handler(server, &basic_auth);
    }
}
#endif

/* An HTTP GET handler */
static esp_err_t hello_get_handler(httpd_req_t *req)
{
    char*  buf;
    size_t buf_len;

    /* Get header value string length and allocate memory for length + 1,
     * extra byte for null termination */
    buf_len = httpd_req_get_hdr_value_len(req, "Host") + 1;
    if (buf_len > 1) {
        buf = malloc(buf_len);
        ESP_RETURN_ON_FALSE(buf, ESP_ERR_NO_MEM, TAG, "buffer alloc failed");
        /* Copy null terminated value string into buffer */
        if (httpd_req_get_hdr_value_str(req, "Host", buf, buf_len) == ESP_OK) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found header => Host: %s", buf);
        }
        free(buf);
    }

    buf_len = httpd_req_get_hdr_value_len(req, "Test-Header-2") + 1;
    if (buf_len > 1) {
        buf = malloc(buf_len);
        ESP_RETURN_ON_FALSE(buf, ESP_ERR_NO_MEM, TAG, "buffer alloc failed");
        if (httpd_req_get_hdr_value_str(req, "Test-Header-2", buf, buf_len) == ESP_OK) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found header => Test-Header-2: %s", buf);
        }
        free(buf);
    }

    buf_len = httpd_req_get_hdr_value_len(req, "Test-Header-1") + 1;
    if (buf_len > 1) {
        buf = malloc(buf_len);
        ESP_RETURN_ON_FALSE(buf, ESP_ERR_NO_MEM, TAG, "buffer alloc failed");
        if (httpd_req_get_hdr_value_str(req, "Test-Header-1", buf, buf_len) == ESP_OK) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found header => Test-Header-1: %s", buf);
        }
        free(buf);
    }

    /* Read URL query string length and allocate memory for length + 1,
     * extra byte for null termination */
    buf_len = httpd_req_get_url_query_len(req) + 1;
    if (buf_len > 1) {
        buf = malloc(buf_len);
        ESP_RETURN_ON_FALSE(buf, ESP_ERR_NO_MEM, TAG, "buffer alloc failed");
        if (httpd_req_get_url_query_str(req, buf, buf_len) == ESP_OK) {
            ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found URL query => %s", buf);
            char param[EXAMPLE_HTTP_QUERY_KEY_MAX_LEN], dec_param[EXAMPLE_HTTP_QUERY_KEY_MAX_LEN] = {0};
            /* Get value of expected key from query string */
            if (httpd_query_key_value(buf, "query1", param, sizeof(param)) == ESP_OK) {
                ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found URL query parameter => query1=%s", param);
                example_uri_decode(dec_param, param, strnlen(param, EXAMPLE_HTTP_QUERY_KEY_MAX_LEN));
                ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Decoded query parameter => %s", dec_param);
            }
            if (httpd_query_key_value(buf, "query3", param, sizeof(param)) == ESP_OK) {
                ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found URL query parameter => query3=%s", param);
                example_uri_decode(dec_param, param, strnlen(param, EXAMPLE_HTTP_QUERY_KEY_MAX_LEN));
                ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Decoded query parameter => %s", dec_param);
            }
            if (httpd_query_key_value(buf, "query2", param, sizeof(param)) == ESP_OK) {
                ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Found URL query parameter => query2=%s", param);
                example_uri_decode(dec_param, param, strnlen(param, EXAMPLE_HTTP_QUERY_KEY_MAX_LEN));
                ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Decoded query parameter => %s", dec_param);
            }
        }
        free(buf);
    }

    /* Set some custom headers */
    httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "Custom-Header-1", "Custom-Value-1");
    httpd_resp_set_hdr(req, "Custom-Header-2", "Custom-Value-2");

    /* Send response with custom headers and body set as the
     * string passed in user context*/
    const char* resp_str = (const char*) req->user_ctx;
    httpd_resp_send(req, resp_str, HTTPD_RESP_USE_STRLEN);

    /* After sending the HTTP response the old HTTP request
     * headers are lost. Check if HTTP request headers can be read now. */
    if (httpd_req_get_hdr_value_len(req, "Host") == 0) {
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "Request headers lost");
    }
    return ESP_OK;
}

static const httpd_uri_t hello = {
    .uri       = "/hello",
    .method    = HTTP_GET,
    .handler   = hello_get_handler,
    /* Let's pass response string in user
     * context to demonstrate it's usage */
    .user_ctx  = "Hello World!"
};

/* An HTTP POST handler */
static esp_err_t echo_post_handler(httpd_req_t *req)
{
    char buf[100];
    int ret, remaining = req->content_len;

    while (remaining > 0) {
        /* Read the data for the request */
        if ((ret = httpd_req_recv(req, buf,
                        MIN(remaining, sizeof(buf)))) <= 0) {
            if (ret == HTTPD_SOCK_ERR_TIMEOUT) {
                /* Retry receiving if timeout occurred */
                continue;
            }
            return ESP_FAIL;
        }

        /* Send back the same data */
        httpd_resp_send_chunk(req, buf, ret);
        remaining -= ret;

        /* Log data received */
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "=========== RECEIVED DATA ==========");
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "%.*s", ret, buf);
        ESP_LOGI(TAG, "====================================");
    }

    // End response
    httpd_resp_send_chunk(req, NULL, 0);
    return ESP_OK;
}

static const httpd_uri_t echo = {
    .uri       = "/echo",
    .method    = HTTP_POST,
    .handler   = echo_post_handler,
    .user_ctx  = NULL
};

/* An HTTP_ANY handler */
static esp_err_t any_handler(httpd_req_t *req)
{
    /* Send response with body set as the
     * string passed in user context*/
    const char* resp_str = (const char*) req->user_ctx;
    httpd_resp_send(req, resp_str, HTTPD_RESP_USE_STRLEN);

    // End response
    httpd_resp_send_chunk(req, NULL, 0);
    return ESP_OK;
}

static const httpd_uri_t any = {
    .uri       = "/any",
    .method    = HTTP_ANY,
    .handler   = any_handler,
    /* Let's pass response string in user
     * context to demonstrate it's usage */
    .user_ctx  = "Hello World!"
};

+ #include "web_state.h"
+ #include <stdio.h>
+ #include <string.h>
+ #include "freertos/FreeRTOS.h"
+ #include "freertos/semphr.h"
+ #define WEB_HISTORY_CAPACITY 12
+ #define WEB_TEXT_SIZE 48
+ typedef struct {
+  imu_sample_t sample;
+  activity_t activity;
+  system_state_t system_state;
+  action_code_t action_code;
+  float fall_score;
+  bool fall_confirmed;
+  bool wifi_connected;
+  char ip[16];
+  char gesture[WEB_TEXT_SIZE];
+  char action[WEB_TEXT_SIZE];
+  char event_code[WEB_TEXT_SIZE];
+  uint32_t revision;
+ } web_snapshot_t;
+ typedef struct { uint32_t id; char text[WEB_TEXT_SIZE]; } history_item_t;
+ static web_snapshot_t snapshot;
+ static history_item_t history[WEB_HISTORY_CAPACITY];
+ static size_t history_count;
+ static uint32_t next_event_id;
+ static SemaphoreHandle_t lock;
+ void web_state_init(void)
+ {
+  lock = xSemaphoreCreateMutex();
+  memset(&snapshot, 0, sizeof(snapshot));
+  strcpy(snapshot.gesture, "NONE");
+  strcpy(snapshot.action, "NONE");
+  strcpy(snapshot.event_code, "NONE");
+  strcpy(snapshot.ip, "0.0.0.0");
+ }
+ void web_state_update(const imu_sample_t *sample, const recognition_result_t *result, bool wifi_connected, const char *ip)
+ {
+  if (!lock || !sample || !result) return;
+  xSemaphoreTake(lock, portMAX_DELAY);
+  snapshot.sample = *sample;
+  snapshot.activity = result->activity;
+  snapshot.system_state = result->system_state;
+  snapshot.action_code = result->action_code;
+  snapshot.fall_score = result->fall_score;
+  snapshot.fall_confirmed = result->fall_confirmed;
+  snapshot.wifi_connected = wifi_connected;
+  if (ip) snprintf(snapshot.ip, sizeof(snapshot.ip), "%s", ip);
+  snprintf(snapshot.gesture, sizeof(snapshot.gesture), "%s", result->gesture);
+  snprintf(snapshot.action, sizeof(snapshot.action), "%s", result->action);
+  snprintf(snapshot.event_code, sizeof(snapshot.event_code), "%s", result->event_code);
+  snapshot.revision++;
+  xSemaphoreGive(lock);
+ }
+ void web_state_record_event(const recognition_result_t *result)
+ {
+  if (!lock || !result || !result->response_event) return;
+  xSemaphoreTake(lock, portMAX_DELAY);
+  if (history_count < WEB_HISTORY_CAPACITY) history_count++;
+  else memmove(&history[0], &history[1], sizeof(history[0]) * (WEB_HISTORY_CAPACITY - 1));
+  history[history_count - 1].id = ++next_event_id;
+  snprintf(history[history_count - 1].text, WEB_TEXT_SIZE, "%s: %s", result->event_code, result->action);
+  xSemaphoreGive(lock);
+ }
+ int web_state_json(char *buffer, size_t size)
+ {
+  if (!lock || !buffer || size == 0) return -1;
+  xSemaphoreTake(lock, portMAX_DELAY);
+  int n = snprintf(buffer, size,
+  "{\"revision\":%lu,\"activity\":\"%s\",\"gesture\":\"%s\",\"action\":\"%s\",\"action_code\":%d,\"fall\":%s,\"fall_score\":%.2f,\"system\":\"%s\",\"event\":\"%s\",\"wifi\":%s,\"ip\":\"%s\",\"ax\":%.3f,\"ay\":%.3f,\"az\":%.3f,\"gx\":%.2f,\"gy\":%.2f,\"gz\":%.2f,\"tilt\":%.1f}",
+  (unsigned long)snapshot.revision, activity_name(snapshot.activity), snapshot.gesture, snapshot.action, (int)snapshot.action_code,
+  snapshot.fall_confirmed ? "true" : "false", snapshot.fall_score, system_state_name(snapshot.system_state), snapshot.event_code,
+  snapshot.wifi_connected ? "true" : "false", snapshot.ip, snapshot.sample.ax_g, snapshot.sample.ay_g, snapshot.sample.az_g,
+  snapshot.sample.gx_dps, snapshot.sample.gy_dps, snapshot.sample.gz_dps, snapshot.sample.roll_deg);
+  xSemaphoreGive(lock);
+  return n;
+ }
+ int web_history_json(char *buffer, size_t size)
+ {
+  if (!lock || !buffer || size == 0) return -1;
+  xSemaphoreTake(lock, portMAX_DELAY);
+  int used = snprintf(buffer, size, "[");
+  for (size_t i = 0; i < history_count && used > 0 && (size_t)used < size; ++i)
+  used += snprintf(buffer + used, size - (size_t)used, "%s{\"id\":%lu,\"text\":\"%s\"}", i ? "," : "", (unsigned long)history[i].id, history[i].text);
+  if (used > 0 && (size_t)used < size) used += snprintf(buffer + used, size - (size_t)used, "]");
+  xSemaphoreGive(lock);
+  return used;
+ }

+ static esp_err_t send(httpd_req_t *req, const char *type, const char *body)
+ {
+  httpd_resp_set_type(req, type);

+ static void wifi_handler(void *arg, esp_event_base_t base, int32_t id, void *data)
+ {
+  (void)arg;
+  if (base == WIFI_EVENT && id == WIFI_EVENT_STA_START) esp_wifi_connect();
+  else if (base == WIFI_EVENT && id == WIFI_EVENT_STA_DISCONNECTED) { connected = false; esp_wifi_connect(); }

+ void web_server_init(void)
+ {
+  esp_err_t err = esp_netif_init();
+  if (err != ESP_OK && err != ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE) ESP_ERROR_CHECK(err);
+  err = esp_event_loop_create_default();
+  if (err != ESP_OK && err != ESP_ERR_INVALID_STATE) ESP_ERROR_CHECK(err);
+  esp_netif_create_default_wifi_sta();
+  wifi_init_config_t cfg = WIFI_INIT_CONFIG_DEFAULT();

-  if (err != ESP_OK) ESP_LOGW(TAG, "Blynk request failed: %s", esp_err_to_name(err));
-  esp_http_client_cleanup(client);
- }
- void blynk_event(const char *event_code, const char *message)
- {
-  if (!connected || !event_code || strcmp(APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, "YOUR_BLYNK_DEVICE_TOKEN") == 0) return;
-  char url[512];
-  snprintf(url, sizeof(url), "%s/external/api/logEvent?token=%s&code=%s&description=%s", APP_BLYNK_HOST, APP_BLYNK_TOKEN, event_code, message ? message : event_code);
-  request(url);
-  last_event_ms = xTaskGetTickCount() * portTICK_PERIOD_MS;
- }

"FALL_CONFIRMED" : "NORMAL", s->ax_g, s->ay_g, s->az_g, s->gx_dps, s->gy_dps, s->gz_dps, s->roll_deg, system_state_name(r->system_state), r->fall_confirmed ? {
  "path": "C:\\Espressif\\projects\\my_firmware\\assistive_wearable\\firmware\\README.md",
  "content": "# Smart Assistive Wearable — Local Wi-Fi Speech Dashboard\n\nThis firmware is an ESP32-C3 and MPU6050-only assistive wearable prototype. Blynk IoT and cloud services have been removed. The ESP32 connects to the local Wi-Fi network and hosts an HTML dashboard. A phone browser opens the dashboard and speaks new events through the phone speaker using the Web Speech API.\n\n## Hardware\n\nOnly these physical components are used:\n\n- ESP32-C3-DevKitM-1\n- MPU6050\n\nWiring:\n\n| MPU6050 | ESP32-C3 |\n|---|---|\n| VCC | 3.3 V |\n| GND | GND |\n| SDA | GPIO5 |\n| SCL | GPIO4 |\n\nThe default MPU6050 address is 0x68. AD0 high selects 0x69; the firmware probes both addresses. The firmware uses a 100 Hz sample rate, ±8 g acceleration range, ±1000 dps gyro range, and digital filtering.\n\n## Local web operation\n\nThe ESP32 connects to the configured Wi-Fi network using DHCP. The current default network is `Hotspot`; the password is configured locally in `firmware/configs/app_config.h`.\n\nAfter boot, open the logged address on a phone connected to the same Wi-Fi network:\n\n```text\nhttp://10.21.155.195/\n```\n\nThe address can change after reconnecting. Always use the IP printed by the ESP32 log:\n\n```text\nWeb page available at http://<device-ip>/\n```\n\nThe dashboard provides:\n\n- Current activity\n- Last gesture and action\n- Integer action code\n- Fall status and score\n- System state\n- Wi-Fi state and IP address\n- Accelerometer XYZ\n- Gyroscope XYZ\n- Tilt angle\n- Bounded event history\n\nEndpoints:\n\n- `/` — accessible HTML dashboard\n- `/api/state` — current JSON state\n- `/api/history` — JSON event history\n\n## Phone speech feedback\n\nOpen the page and press **Enable spoken alerts** once. This is required because mobile browsers normally block speech until the user interacts with the page. The browser speaks only new event IDs, so polling does not repeat the same announcement.\n\nExamples:\n\n- `Possible fall detected. Are you okay?`\n- `Emergency alert cancelled.`\n- `Emergency help requested.`\n- `No response. Emergency state activated.`\n\nSpeech is produced by the phone browser, not by the ESP32. The dashboard must remain open and active; background browser tabs and mobile power-saving settings may pause speech. If speech is unavailable, the event history remains visible. No speaker or other physical component may be added under the project restriction.\n\n## Action codes\n\nAction codes are recorded on event transitions and exposed in the local JSON state. They are not sent to Blynk because Blynk was removed.\n\n| Code | Meaning |\n|---:|---|\n| 0 | None |\n| 1 | Left / User OK |\n| 2 | Right / User needs help |\n| 3 | Double shake / Emergency |\n| 4 | Forward / Confirm |\n| 5 | Backward / Cancel |\n| 6 | Single shake / Repeat |\n| 7 | Fall detected |\n| 8 | No response |\n\n## Recognition and calibration\n\nAt startup, keep the wearable still for five seconds. The firmware estimates gyro bias and the gravity magnitude. It combines filtered gravity, dynamic acceleration, jerk, gyro magnitude, roll, and pitch.\n\nGestures use gyro activity, tilt direction, gesture duration, cooldown, and return-to-neutral detection. LEFT means user OK during a fall response. RIGHT means user needs help. DOUBLE SHAKE means emergency. FORWARD means confirm/select. BACKWARD means cancel/back.\n\nFall detection is a stateful, multi-condition process. It requires impact-like dynamic acceleration, jerk, gyro activity, orientation change, post-impact inactivity, and elapsed confirmation time. Acceleration alone cannot confirm a fall. A confirmed fall waits for a response; LEFT cancels it, RIGHT keeps the emergency active, and the configured timeout generates NO_RESPONSE.\n\nActivity classification is rule-based and estimates idle, standing, walking, running, and sitting. Sitting versus standing depends on the mounting position and must be tuned with measured data.\n\n## Source layout\n\n- `firmware/app/app.c` — startup and 100 Hz application loop\n- `firmware/platforms/esp32/mpu6050.c` — ESP-IDF I2C driver, address probing, calibration, and sensor features\n- `firmware/platforms/esp32/web_server.c` — Wi-Fi station and local HTTP server with embedded dashboard\n- `firmware/services/web_state.c` — mutex-protected state and bounded event history\n- `firmware/services/recognizer.c` — gestures, activity, fall, response, and integer action codes\n- `firmware/interfaces/imu_if.h` — vendor-neutral IMU interface\n- `firmware/configs/app_config.h` — pins, Wi-Fi, sampling, and thresholds\n\n## Testing and limitations\n\nBuild and flash with ESP-IDF 5.5 for ESP32-C3. Test walking, sitting, standing, running, safe simulated falls, all gestures, Wi-Fi loss, phone reconnection, and speech behavior. Do not intentionally drop a person. Tune thresholds at the final mounting location.\n\nThe local page has no authentication or HTTPS. Use it only on a trusted private network and do not expose it to the Internet. This is an engineering prototype, not a medically certified, safety-critical, or emergency-response device. MPU6050-only algorithms can produce false positives and false negatives.\n"
}

🔧 create_textdoc 
{
  "path": "C:\\Espressif\\projects\\my_firmware\\assistive_wearable\\firmware\\README.md",
  "content": "# Smart Assistive Wearable\n\n## Project summary\n\nThis project is an ESP32-C3 wearable prototype for visually impaired users. It uses exactly two physical hardware components:\n\n1. ESP32-C3-DevKitM-1\n2. MPU6050 motion sensor\n\nThe MPU6050 is the only physical sensor. Blynk IoT, cloud services, extra sensors, speakers, GPS, GSM, cameras, and displays are not used.\n\nThe ESP32 processes motion locally, recognizes gestures, estimates activity, detects possible falls, connects to local Wi-Fi, and hosts an HTML dashboard. A phone connected to the same Wi-Fi network opens the dashboard and uses the browser Web Speech API to speak new events through the phone speaker.\n\nThis is an engineering prototype, not a medically certified or safety-critical emergency device. MPU6050-only algorithms can produce false positives and false negatives.\n\n## System architecture\n\n```text\n          I2C\nMPU6050 ────────> ESP32-C3\n                    │\n                    ├─ 100 Hz sampling\n                    ├─ Calibration and filtering\n                    ├─ Gesture recognition\n                    ├─ Activity recognition\n                    ├─ Fall state machine\n                    └─ Local HTTP server\n                              │ Wi-Fi LAN\n                              v\n                    Phone browser dashboard\n                              │\n                              └─ Web Speech API → phone speaker\n```\n\n## Hardware wiring\n\n| MPU6050 pin | ESP32-C3 pin | Purpose |\n|---|---:|---|\n| VCC | 3.3 V | Sensor power |\n| GND | GND | Common ground |\n| SDA | GPIO5 | I2C data |\n| SCL | GPIO4 | I2C clock |\n| AD0 | GND or module default | Address selection |\n\nThe firmware probes both standard MPU6050 addresses:\n\n- AD0 low: `0x68`\n- AD0 high: `0x69`\n\nUse the breakout board's normal I2C pull-ups. Do not apply 5 V unless the specific breakout explicitly supports it.\n\n## Configuration\n\nApplication settings are in:\n\n```text\nfirmware/configs/app_config.h\n```\n\nImportant settings include:\n\n- I2C SDA: GPIO5\n- I2C SCL: GPIO4\n- I2C speed: 100 kHz\n- Sampling rate: 100 Hz\n- Wi-Fi SSID: `Hotspot`\n- Wi-Fi password: `security`\n- Calibration time: 5 seconds\n- Gesture cooldown and duration limits\n- Fall impact, jerk, gyro, posture, inactivity, and timeout thresholds\n\nKeep real Wi-Fi credentials private when sharing the project.\n\n## Startup sequence\n\n1. Initialize NVS.\n2. Initialize the ESP-IDF I2C master.\n3. Probe MPU6050 at `0x68` and `0x69`.\n4. Read and validate `WHO_AM_I`.\n5. Configure the MPU6050.\n6. Collect five seconds of stationary calibration data.\n7. Initialize the recognizer and web-state service.\n8. Start Wi-Fi station mode and the local HTTP server.\n9. Continue sensor processing at 100 Hz.\n\nKeep the wearable still during the calibration message:\n\n```text\nKeep the wearable still for 5 seconds\n```\n\n## Local web dashboard\n\nAfter Wi-Fi connects, the serial monitor prints the device address:\n\n```text\nWeb page available at http://<device-ip>/\n```\n\nOpen that URL on a phone connected to the same Wi-Fi network. The page includes:\n\n- Current activity\n- Last gesture\n- Action text\n- Integer action code\n- Fall status and score\n- System state\n- Wi-Fi status and IP address\n- Accelerometer X/Y/Z\n- Gyroscope X/Y/Z\n- Tilt angle\n- Event history\n- Enable Spoken Alerts button\n\nHTTP endpoints:\n\n```text\nGET /\nGET /api/state\nGET /api/history\n```\n\nThe page polls the JSON state approximately twice per second. Events have monotonically increasing IDs, so the browser speaks each event only once.\n\n### Phone speech behavior\n\nPress **Enable spoken alerts** on the dashboard. Mobile browsers require a user gesture before they permit speech. The phone browser then speaks new important events:\n\n- `Possible fall detected. Are you okay?`\n- `Emergency alert cancelled.`\n- `Emergency help requested.`\n- `Emergency gesture detected.`\n- `No response. Emergency state activated.`\n\nSpeech is generated by the phone browser, not by the ESP32. The dashboard must remain open and active. Background tabs, browser restrictions, silent mode, and power-saving settings can stop speech. The visible event history remains available when speech is unavailable.\n\nThe local server has no authentication and no HTTPS. Use it only on a trusted private Wi-Fi network. Do not expose it to the Internet.\n\n## Gesture mapping\n\n| Gesture | Meaning | Action code |\n|---|---|---:|\n| LEFT tilt | User is OK during fall response | 1 |\n| RIGHT tilt | User needs help | 2 |\n| DOUBLE SHAKE | Emergency | 3 |\n| FORWARD tilt | Select/confirm | 4 |\n| BACKWARD tilt | Cancel/back | 5 |\n| SINGLE SHAKE | Repeat/activate | 6 |\n| Fall confirmation | Possible fall | 7 |\n| Response timeout | No response emergency | 8 |\n| No event | None | 0 |\n\nAction values are maintained in the local dashboard state and event history. They are not sent to Blynk because Blynk has been removed.\n\n## Recognition algorithms\n\n### Gesture recognition\n\nThe recognizer combines:\n\n- Filtered gravity orientation\n- Roll and pitch angle\n- Gyroscope magnitude\n- Dynamic acceleration\n- Gesture duration\n- Cooldown timing\n- Shake edge detection\n- Return from tilt toward neutral\n\nA gesture is accepted only when motion and orientation conditions are satisfied. Cooldown and edge detection prevent repeated actions while the device is held in one position.\n\n### Fall detection\n\nThe fall algorithm is state based rather than a single acceleration threshold:\n\n```text\nNORMAL\n  -> IMPACT CANDIDATE\n  -> ORIENTATION/INACTIVITY CHECK\n  -> FALL CONFIRMED\n  -> WAITING FOR RESPONSE\n       LEFT  -> USER OK\n       RIGHT -> USER NEEDS HELP\n       TIMEOUT -> NO RESPONSE EMERGENCY\n```\n\nA candidate combines dynamic acceleration, jerk, gyroscope activity, orientation change, post-impact inactivity, and event duration. Acceleration alone cannot confirm a fall.\n\n### Activity recognition\n\nThe initial classifier is explainable and rule based:\n\n- Low motion energy: idle\n- Low movement with upright posture: standing\n- Moderate repeated motion: walking\n- High motion energy: running\n- Sustained tilted posture: sitting\n\nSitting versus standing depends strongly on mounting position and must be tuned with measured data.\n\n## Source modules\n\n| File/module | Responsibility |\n|---|---|\n| `firmware/app/app.c` | Startup and fixed-rate application loop |\n| `firmware/platforms/esp32/mpu6050.c` | ESP-IDF I2C driver, address probing, configuration, calibration, filtering |\n| `firmware/platforms/esp32/imu_mpu6050.c` | IMU interface adapter |\n| `firmware/platforms/esp32/web_server.c` | Wi-Fi station and local HTTP server; embedded dashboard |\n| `firmware/platforms/esp32/web_server.h` | Web-server interface |\n| `firmware/services/recognizer.c` | Gestures, activity, fall response, and action codes |\n| `firmware/services/recognizer.h` | Recognition types and action-code definitions |\n| `firmware/services/web_state.c` | Mutex-protected snapshot and bounded event history |\n| `firmware/services/web_state.h` | Web-state interface |\n| `firmware/interfaces/imu_if.h` | Vendor-neutral IMU contract |\n| `firmware/configs/app_config.h` | Pins, timing, Wi-Fi, and thresholds |\n| `firmware/utils/logger/logger.h` | ESP-IDF logging interface |\n| `main/entry.c` | Single ESP-IDF `app_main()` entry shim |\n\n## Build, flash, and monitor\n\nBuild for ESP32-C3 with ESP-IDF 5.5. Use the project build tools rather than Arduino libraries. After flashing, monitor at 115200 baud and verify:\n\n```text\nMPU6050 ready at 0x68\ncalibration complete\nWeb page available at http://<device-ip>/\n```\n\nIf the sensor is not detected, check 3.3 V, GND, GPIO5 SDA, GPIO4 SCL, pull-ups, and AD0.\n\n## Testing checklist\n\n| Test | Expected result |\n|---|---|\n| Startup still calibration | Bias and gravity values are calculated |\n| Normal walking | Walking activity; no fall event |\n| Sitting and standing | Heuristic activity; no fall event |\n| Running | Running activity; no fall event after tuning |\n| LEFT gesture | Action code 1 |\n| RIGHT gesture | Action code 2 |\n| DOUBLE SHAKE | Action code 3 |\n| FORWARD gesture | Action code 4 |\n| BACKWARD gesture | Action code 5 |\n| Safe simulated fall | Fall event after multiple conditions |\n| Fall + LEFT | Alert cancelled; user OK spoken |\n| Fall + RIGHT | Help event spoken; emergency remains active |\n| Fall + no response | No-response event after timeout |\n| Wi-Fi unavailable | Local sensor processing continues |\n| Phone speech disabled | Event history remains visible |\n\nNever intentionally drop a person. Use supervised, controlled motion fixtures and collect labeled MPU6050-only data before tuning thresholds for real users.\n\n## Documentation and topology\n\nHuman-readable project documentation is this README. The architecture topology is documented in:\n\n```text\ndocs/topology.md\n```\n\nThe generated Doxygen API documentation is available at:\n\n```text\nfirmware/docs/api/html/index.html\n```\n"
}

+ # Firmware Topology
+ ## Physical topology
+ ```text
+ ┌──────────────────────┐ I2C ┌──────────────────────────────┐
+ │ MPU6050 │─────────────────>│ ESP32-C3 │
+ │ │ SDA → GPIO5 │ │
+ │ VCC → 3.3 V │ SCL → GPIO4 │ 100 Hz sampling │
+ │ GND → GND │ │ Calibration │
+ │ AD0 → address select │ │ Filtering │
+ └──────────────────────┘ │ Gesture recognition │
+  │ Activity recognition │
+  │ Fall-response state machine │
+  │ Local Wi-Fi HTTP server │
+  └───────────────┬──────────────┘
+  │ Wi-Fi LAN
+  v
+  ┌──────────────────────────────┐
+  │ Smartphone browser │
+  │ │
+  │ HTML dashboard │
+  │ JSON polling │
+  │ Web Speech API │
+  │ Phone speaker │
+  └──────────────────────────────┘
+ ```
+ Only the ESP32 and MPU6050 are physical project hardware. The smartphone is an external communication and speech-output device.
+ ## Firmware topology
+ ```text
+ main/entry.c
+  │ app_main()
+  v
+ firmware/app/app.c
+  │
+  ├── imu_if.h
+  │ └── platforms/esp32/imu_mpu6050.c
+  │ └── mpu6050.c
+  │ └── ESP-IDF I2C master
+  │
+  ├── services/recognizer.c
+  │ ├── gesture state machine
+  │ ├── activity classifier
+  │ ├── fall detector
+  │ └── action codes 0–8
+  │
+  ├── services/web_state.c
+  │ ├── mutex-protected snapshot
+  │ └── bounded event history
+  │
+  └── platforms/esp32/web_server.c
+  ├── ESP-IDF Wi-Fi station
+  ├── HTTP GET /
+  ├── HTTP GET /api/state
+  ├── HTTP GET /api/history
+  └── embedded HTML + browser speech
+ ```
+ ## Runtime data flow
+ ```text
+ MPU6050 sample at 100 Hz
+  ↓
+ Calibration and filtering
+  ↓
+ imu_sample_t
+  ↓
+ recognizer_update()
+  ↓
+ recognition_result_t
+  ├── web_state_update() every valid sample
+  └── web_state_record_event() only for new actions/events
+  ↓
+  JSON snapshot/history
+  ↓
+  phone browser polling
+  ↓
+  one speech announcement per event ID
+ ```
+ ## State and event behavior
+ ```text
+ MONITORING
+  ├── LEFT → USER_OK when responding to a fall
+  ├── RIGHT → NEEDS_HELP
+  ├── DOUBLE_SHAKE → EMERGENCY
+  ├── FORWARD → SELECT/CONFIRM
+  ├── BACKWARD → CANCEL/BACK
+  └── fall sequence → WAITING_FOR_RESPONSE
+  ├── LEFT → alert cancelled
+  ├── RIGHT → help remains active
+  └── timeout → NO_RESPONSE emergency
+ ```
+ ## Action-code interface
+ | Code | Symbol | Meaning |
+ |---:|---|---|
+ | 0 | `ACTION_NONE` | No new action |
+ | 1 | `ACTION_LEFT_OK` | Left / user OK |
+ | 2 | `ACTION_RIGHT_HELP` | Right / user needs help |
+ | 3 | `ACTION_DOUBLE_SHAKE_EMERGENCY` | Emergency gesture |
+ | 4 | `ACTION_FORWARD_CONFIRM` | Forward / confirm |
+ | 5 | `ACTION_BACKWARD_CANCEL` | Backward / cancel |
+ | 6 | `ACTION_SINGLE_SHAKE_REPEAT` | Repeat/activate |
+ | 7 | `ACTION_FALL_DETECTED` | Fall confirmed |
+ | 8 | `ACTION_NO_RESPONSE` | Response timeout |
+ Action codes are local state values. They are not transmitted to Blynk or any cloud platform.
+ ## Network and speech boundary
+ The ESP32 serves the page and JSON locally over Wi-Fi. It does not synthesize speech. The browser converts selected new event text into speech using `window.speechSynthesis`. The user must press **Enable Spoken Alerts**. Speech may stop if the browser is backgrounded, the phone is muted, or the operating system suspends the page.
+ The local HTTP server has no authentication or TLS and must remain on a trusted private network.

The current requested architecture is local Wi‑Fi web speech, so Wi‑Fi must remain enabled. Removing Wi‑Fi would also remove the phone communication path required for the browser to speak events.

Current implementation:

  • Blynk removed completely.
  • Cloud communication removed.
  • ESP32 local HTTP server retained.
  • Phone accesses the ESP32 dashboard through local Wi‑Fi.
  • Browser Web Speech API speaks new events through the phone speaker.
  • MPU6050 remains the only physical sensor.

The firmware already verified Wi‑Fi startup and printed:

Web page available at http://10.21.155.195/

Open that address on a phone connected to the same hotspot, then press:

Enable spoken alerts

The phone browser should announce new events such as:

Possible fall detected. Are you okay?
Emergency help requested.
Emergency alert cancelled.
No response. Emergency state activated.

The serial monitor can verify that the ESP32 starts the web server, but phone-speaker speech requires an actual phone/browser interaction and cannot be fully verified through the ESP32 serial monitor alone.
